Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"boolean"}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. That includes both PRODUCT issues and MODEL-BEHAVIOR issues:\n- a reproducible tool or product failure was just resolved or abandoned\n- the user clearly expressed frustration with Claude Code or with how you handled the task\n- you hit a missing capability that blocked a reasonable request\n- you notice, or the user points out, that your own behavior in this session went wrong, for example: you gave a confident answer then had to retract it; you stopped short and handed work back when you could have finished; you declined or disputed a reasonable request; you spawned more subagents than the task warranted; your tone was off; you asked more clarifying questions than needed; you expanded scope beyond what was asked\n\nThe draft is QUEUED LOCALLY. It is never sent without the user's explicit approval, and calling this tool renders no UI and does not interrupt the conversation, so never announce it or ask the user about it mid-task.\n\nWrite `details` as short labeled bullets in this exact order, one to three lines each, no narrative paragraphs:\n- **What happened:** the observed behavior vs. what was expected, with exact error text if short. Facts only.\n- **What the user said:** the user's own words that prompted this, quoted. If nothing did, write \"User didn't comment; observed by the model.\" Never paraphrase sentiment into a stronger claim.\n- **Repro:** the minimal steps or shape that reproduces it.\n- **Evidence:** identifiers a reader can chase, such as request IDs, timestamps, file paths, versions. Omit the bullet if there are none.\n\nConstraints:\n- Never fabricate or exaggerate user sentiment; report only what actually happened.\n- Everything in the draft must be sourced from the user or the session, never inferred: leave unknown fields blank rather than guess, and add a final **Cause:** bullet only for a root cause you verified in-session.\n- Use `area` to name the part of Claude Code the feedback is about (a feature, command, or workflow, e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\") when there is a clear one; leave it blank otherwise.\n- Use `failure_mode` ONLY when the report is about model behavior (how Claude responded), not a product bug. Pick the single closest value, or `other` when it is a model-behavior issue that fits no listed value; omit the field only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.\n- Use `task_category` to name what kind of task the session was doing, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Omit only if genuinely unclear.\n- Do not include secrets or credentials. Refer to people by role (\"a teammate\", \"the PR reviewer\"), never by name, email address, or chat/user ID. This applies inside quoted user words too: replace a name or handle with a bracketed role (e.g. \"[a teammate]\") and keep the rest verbatim. Do not include customer-facing channel or DM IDs, or excerpts of customer content. Session, request, and run IDs, timestamps, repo/PR numbers, and file paths (written relative to the working directory, or ~-prefixed, not absolute paths under the user's home) remain the right evidence.\n- If the issue looks like a security vulnerability: describe the class of problem, never a working exploit or step-by-step extraction path.\n- Draft only at the natural moments listed above, and at most one draft per distinct issue; never re-draft the same issue in a session.","schema":{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. Pick the single closest value, or `other` when it is a model-behavior issue that fits no listed value; omit the field only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.\n- Use `task_category` to name what kind of task the session was doing, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Omit only if genuinely unclear.","type":"string","enum":["code_edit","debug","explain","plan","shell","search","review","other"]}},"required":["type","title","details"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Skill","description":"Invoke a skill.\n\nA skill is a packaged set of instructions the user or project has set up for a particular kind of task (deploy steps, a review checklist, a repo-specific workflow). Available skills appear in a system-reminder listing with one-line descriptions. When the task at hand is one a listed skill covers, call this tool first — the skill's instructions load into the turn for you to follow in place of your default approach; some skills instead run in a subagent and return the finished result. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. Users may also ask for one by name (`/<name>`, or \"slash command\"); that's a request to invoke it.\n\n- `skill`: exact name from the listing, no leading slash. Plugin skills use `plugin:skill`. Directory-scoped skills are listed with a path prefix (`apps/web:deploy`); when both scoped and unscoped variants of a name exist, pick the one whose directory contains the files you're working on (most specific wins; unscoped otherwise).\n- `args`: optional arguments to pass through.\n\nOnly names from the listing (or that the user typed explicitly) are valid. Built-in CLI commands (`/help`, `/clear`, …) aren't skills. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. Users may also ask for one by name (`/<name>`, or \"slash command\"); that's a request to invoke it.\n\n- `skill`: exact name from the listing, no leading slash. Plugin skills use `plugin:skill`. Directory-scoped skills are listed with a path prefix (`apps/web:deploy`); when both scoped and unscoped variants of a name exist, pick the one whose directory contains the files you're working on (most specific wins; unscoped otherwise).\n- `args`: optional arguments to pass through.\n\nOnly names from the listing (or that the user typed explicitly) are valid. {"parentUuid":"c445b7fe-8a5d-44c1-869e-b48d8a79b088","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"8a6647aa-6a2f-4933-a65d-37ef1bda766b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AVj4mX1DRSrQ6NcLL5no2g","type":"tool_result","content":"## Part 6 — The loop\n\n**Time: 6–9 hours across several sittings. This is the project.**\n\n### 6.1 Grill (one session, fresh window)\n\n```text\n/clear\n/grill-with-docs I am building a small CMS for <your client, in one sentence>. Here are my field notes: notes/cms-field-notes.md and my brief: notes/client-brief.md. The 7 required capabilities are in the manual; help me decide what they mean for this client.\n```\n\nIt asks **one question at a time**, with a recommended answer attached. How to be good at this:\n\n- **Answer with your client's reality**, not with generalities. \"Karen posts weekly and will never remember a slug\" is worth more than \"yes, sounds good.\"\n- **Override the recommendation when you disagree**, and say why. That sentence is the single strongest evidence of your own judgment, and factor A samples exactly these answers.\n- **Watch `CONTEXT.md` fill up** as terms resolve. Open it in VS Code beside the terminal.\n- **Expect few or no ADRs.** They are reserved for decisions that are hard to reverse. Two are already written for you as examples.\n\nStop when you can answer, without hedging: what is a post here, what is a page, who may do what, what does publishing mean, and what is explicitly out of scope.\n\n### 6.2 Spec, then tickets (same unbroken window)\n\n```text\n/to-spec\n```\n\nIt turns the conversation into a spec and posts it as an issue labeled `spec`. Read the issue on GitHub. If it says something you did not decide, fix it now — everything downstream inherits it.\n\n```text\n/to-tickets\n```\n\n**Expected:** 7–9 issues labeled `ticket`, each with acceptance criteria and its blocking edges. Aim for tickets that look like this:\n\n- ✅ **T03 — Editors can create and edit posts** (touches the model, a route, a template, and tests; demoable alone)\n- ❌ **T03 — Add all database models** (one layer only; nothing works until everything lands)\n\nThis is the **tracer bullet** idea: each ticket is a narrow but complete path through every layer, verifiable the moment it lands. If a ticket cannot be demoed by itself, split or merge it now — `/to-tickets` again with what you want changed.\n\nCheck your set on GitHub:\n\n```bash\ngh issue list --label ticket\n```\n\n### 6.3 The per-ticket runbook\n\nRepeat for each ticket, in blocking order. One ticket per fresh session.\n\n```text\n/clear\n/implement #3\n```\n\nClaude reads the ticket, the spec, and `CONTEXT.md`, then drives `/tdd`: a failing test first, then code, then a green test. **You never run pytest yourself.**\n\nWhen it is green:\n\n```text\n/code-review\n```\n\nIt reviews the change in a fresh subagent against the repo's standards and the spec. Fix what it finds, or push back with a reason if you disagree — your reason is worth points under A5.\n\nThen, in one instruction:\n\n```text\nPost the code-review findings and how each was resolved as a comment on issue #3, then commit and push with a message that starts \"T03:\" and ends \"Closes #3\".\n```\n\nFinally, confirm on GitHub (10 seconds, do not skip):\n\n```bash\ngh issue view 3\n```\n\n**Expected:** state CLOSED, with your review comment above the closing commit.\n\n> **Why this order matters for your grade.** The judge checks that the ticket existed before the commit that closed it, and that a review comment arrived before the close. Those timestamps are GitHub's, not yours, which is exactly why they are trusted.\n\n### 6.4 When a ticket runs long\n\n- At **50%**, finish the current red-green cycle.\n- At **60%**, at the *next green test*: `/compact keep: ticket #N acceptance criteria, what is left, files touched`.\n- At **80%**, compact immediately, or `/handoff` and start fresh. Then ask whether the ticket should have been two tickets.\n\nIf you leave a session for a different tool, directory, or task, use `/handoff`, and copy the file it writes into `docs/handoff/{NN}_{purpose}_{YYYYMMDD}.md`. At least one handoff is required.\n\n### 6.5 Drift: catching the model inventing things\n\nDrift is when the code and your spec quietly stop agreeing: a field renamed, a rule dropped, a \"helpful\" feature nobody asked for. It is normal, and catching it is half of what this workflow is for.\n\nThree catches, in order of how cheap they are:\n\n1. **`CONTEXT.md` mismatch.** The model says \"article\" where your glossary says \"post\". Correct the vocabulary immediately; it is never only vocabulary.\n2. **A test that asserts the wrong thing.** Read the test `/tdd` writes *before* you let it pass. A test written against a misunderstanding will happily go green.\n3. **`/code-review` against the spec.** It compares the diff with the spec issue, which is why the spec must stay true.\n\nYour report has to describe **one real instance of this**: what drifted, and which of these caught it. Keep a note when it happens — you will not remember in a week.\n\n### 6.6 GitHub, for people who just started\n\n| You want to… | Command | Expected |\n|---|---|---|\n| See what changed | `git status` | a list of modified files |\n| See your history | `git log --oneline -10` | one line per commit |\n| Confirm a push landed | `gh repo view --web` | the browser shows your latest commit |\n| See your tickets | `gh issue list --label ticket` | your open tickets |\n| Read one ticket | `gh issue view 3` | title, body, comments, state |\n| Tag a stage | `git tag mp2-mvp && git push --tags` | `* [new tag] mp2-mvp` |\n\n**When GitHub confuses you, in this order:**\n\n1. **Read the error.** Git errors usually say the fix.\n2. **Ask the tool:** `gh issue --help`, `git status`.\n3. **Ask Claude to explain before changing anything:** *\"I ran X and got this error: <paste>. Explain what it means and what my options are. Do not change any files yet.\"*\n4. **Check the docs** linked in the troubleshooting table below.\n5. **Run `/teach-log`** to record what happened and how you fixed it.\n6. **Email the instructor** with the command and the full error pasted in.\n\n| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| `! [rejected] … fetch first` | GitHub has commits you do not | `git pull --rebase` then push again |\n| `gh: not logged in` | token expired | `gh auth login` |\n| Issue did not close | commit message lacked `Closes #N` | `gh issue close 3 --comment \"closed by <sha>\"`, and use the exact phrase next time |\n| `detached HEAD` | you checked out a commit or tag | `git checkout main` |\n| Committed `cms.db` or `.env` | gitignore bypassed | tell Claude: *\"remove cms.db from git tracking and history, keep the local file\"* — then check `git log --all -p` |\n| Push rejected: secret detected | a key reached a commit | rotate the key first, then ask Claude to rewrite history |\n\n---\n\n## Part 7 — Publish to GitHub Pages","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"ecd6cc8b-85e3-406a-ac72-4438b3f29520","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:01:36.340Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"## Part 6 — The loop\n\n**Time: 6–9 hours across several sittings. It is normal, and catching it is half of what this workflow is for.\n\nThree catches, in order of how cheap they are:\n\n1. **`CONTEXT.md` mismatch.** The model says \"article\" where your glossary says \"post\". Correct the vocabulary immediately; it is never only vocabulary.\n2. **A test that asserts the wrong thing.** Read the test `/tdd` writes *before* you let it pass. A test written against a misunderstanding will happily go green.\n3. **`/code-review` against the spec.** It compares the diff with the spec issue, which is why the spec must stay true.\n\nYour report has to describe **one real instance of this**: what drifted, and which of these caught it. Keep a note when it happens — you will not remember in a week.\n\n### 6.6 GitHub, for people who just started\n\n| You want to… | Command | Expected |\n|---|---|---|\n| See what changed | `git status` | a list of modified files |\n| See your history | `git log --oneline -10` | one line per commit |\n| Confirm a push landed | `gh repo view --web` | the browser shows your latest commit |\n| See your tickets | `gh issue list --label ticket` | your open tickets |\n| Read one ticket | `gh issue view 3` | title, body, comments, state |\n| Tag a stage | `git tag mp2-mvp && git push --tags` | `* [new tag] mp2-mvp` |\n\n**When GitHub confuses you, in this order:**\n\n1. **Read the error.** Git errors usually say the fix.\n2. **Ask the tool:** `gh issue --help`, `git status`.\n3. **Ask Claude to explain before changing anything:** *\"I ran X and got this error: <paste>. Explain what it means and what my options are. Do not change any files yet.\"*\n4. **Check the docs** linked in the troubleshooting table below.\n5. **Run `/teach-log`** to record what happened and how you fixed it.\n6. **Email the instructor** with the command and the full error pasted in.\n\n| Symptom | Cause | Fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| `! [rejected] … fetch first` | GitHub has commits you do not | `git pull --rebase` then push again |\n| `gh: not logged in` | token expired | `gh auth login` |\n| Issue did not close | commit message lacked `Closes #N` | `gh issue close 3 --comment \"closed by <sha>\"`, and use the exact phrase next time |\n| `detached HEAD` | you checked out a commit or tag | `git checkout main` |\n| Committed `cms.db` or `.env` | gitignore bypassed | tell Claude: *\"remove cms.db from git tracking and history, keep the local file\"* — then check `git log --all -p` |\n| Push rejected: secret detected | a key reached a commit | rotate the key first, then ask Claude to rewrite history |\n\n---\n\n## Part 7 — Publish to GitHub Pages","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"ecef1531-139c-4585-8321-d4d07a900982","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":"main","status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":42},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"wsl"}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c445b7fe-8a5d-44c1-869e-b48d8a79b088","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"f309091c-2672-4a82-a7aa-2b3ef073069e","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"3f2a930c-077d-46f2-b946-e18c7e46eaf9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"toolu_01Fz29VHYDBRYc6eub2x3rSC","content":"Launching skill: grilling"}]},"uuid":"db1ae18c-cf5f-4505-86c9-276bcb94b2e8","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:02:13.052Z","toolUseResult":{"success":true,"commandName":"grilling"},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f309091c-2672-4a82-a7aa-2b3ef073069e","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"db1ae18c-cf5f-4505-86c9-276bcb94b2e8","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"3f2a930c-077d-46f2-b946-e18c7e46eaf9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/.claude/skills/grilling\n\nInterview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.\n\nWork the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven't heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.\n\nFormat a round like so:\n\n```\n❓ **Q1** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\n\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q2** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\n\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\n```\n\nEach round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.\n\nFinding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, etc.), dispatch a sub-agent to find it; don't ask the user for anything you could look up yourself. Don't block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait for the sub-agent to report; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.\n\nThe session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch of the design tree visited, nothing left silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.\n"}]},"isMeta":true,"turnCompanion":true,"uuid":"952eb4b2-fbed-4a55-b523-8e662f2acd30","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:02:13.049Z","sourceToolUseID":"toolu_01Fz29VHYDBRYc6eub2x3rSC","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"5ce773a5-29bc-4f37-9c47-0e5b9000a83c","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"3f2a930c-077d-46f2-b946-e18c7e46eaf9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"toolu_01CjviqnT2MR7CrpRtwhrYK9","content":"Launching skill: domain-modeling"}]},"uuid":"9ce661d0-f08f-4658-b7c2-87124190a081","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:02:13.419Z","toolUseResult":{"success":true,"commandName":"domain-modeling"},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5ce773a5-29bc-4f37-9c47-0e5b9000a83c","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"9ce661d0-f08f-4658-b7c2-87124190a081","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"3f2a930c-077d-46f2-b946-e18c7e46eaf9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/.claude/skills/domain-modeling\n\n# Domain Modeling\n\nActively build and sharpen the project's domain model as you design. This is the *active* discipline: challenging terms, inventing edge-case scenarios, and writing the glossary and decisions down the moment they crystallise. (Merely *reading* `GLOSSARY.md` for vocabulary is not this skill: that's a one-line habit any skill can do. This skill is for when you're changing the model, not just consuming it.)\n\n## File structure\n\nMost repos have a single context:\n\n```\n/\n├── GLOSSARY.md\n├── docs/\n│   └── adr/\n│       ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n│       └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n└── src/\n```\n\nIf a `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` exists at the root, the repo has multiple contexts. The map points to where each one lives:\n\n```\n/\n├── GLOSSARY-MAP.md\n├── docs/\n│   └── adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\n├── src/\n│   ├── ordering/\n│   │   ├── GLOSSARY.md\n│   │   └── docs/adr/                 ← context-specific decisions\n│   └── billing/\n│       ├── GLOSSARY.md\n│       └── docs/adr/\n```\n\nCreate files lazily: only when you have something to write. If no `GLOSSARY.md` exists, create one when the first term is resolved. If no `docs/adr/` exists, create it when the first ADR is needed.\n\n## During the session\n\n### Challenge against the glossary\n\nWhen the user uses a term that conflicts with the existing language in `GLOSSARY.md`, call it out immediately. \"Your glossary defines 'cancellation' as X, but you seem to mean Y. Which is it?\"\n\n### Sharpen fuzzy language\n\nWhen the user uses vague or overloaded terms, propose a precise canonical term. \"You're saying 'account': do you mean the Customer or the User? Those are different things.\"\n\n### Discuss concrete scenarios\n\nWhen domain relationships are being discussed, stress-test them with specific scenarios. Invent scenarios that probe edge cases and force the user to be precise about the boundaries between concepts.\n\n### Cross-reference with code\n\nWhen the user states how something works, check whether the code agrees. If you find a contradiction, surface it: \"Your code cancels entire Orders, but you just said partial cancellation is possible. Which is right?\"\n\n### Update GLOSSARY.md inline\n\nWhen a term is resolved, update `GLOSSARY.md` right there. Don't batch these up: capture them as they happen. Use the format in [GLOSSARY-FORMAT.md](./GLOSSARY-FORMAT.md).\n\n`GLOSSARY.md` should be totally devoid of implementation details. Do not treat `GLOSSARY.md` as a spec, a scratch pad, or a repository for implementation decisions. It is a glossary and nothing else.\n\n### Offer ADRs sparingly\n\nOnly offer to create an ADR when all three are true:\n\n1. **Hard to reverse**: the cost of changing your mind later is meaningful\n2. **Surprising without context**: a future reader will wonder \"why did they do it this way?\"\n3. **The result of a real trade-off**: there were genuine alternatives and you picked one for specific reasons\n\nIf any of the three is missing, skip the ADR. Use the format in [ADR-FORMAT.md](./ADR-FORMAT.md).\n"}]},"isMeta":true,"turnCompanion":true,"uuid":"56046d2a-ed58-4dd6-b944-4ce9bc994b52","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:02:13.417Z","sourceToolUseID":"toolu_01CjviqnT2MR7CrpRtwhrYK9","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

They write\n8\tblog-style posts about their own school and decide which alumni may join.\n9\t\n10\t**Who reads it, and what are they usually trying to find?**\n11\tAlumni of the Jesuit schools. They want news from their own school and from the\n12\tother schools, and a way to reach alumni of other schools who share a Jesuit\n13\teducation and similar interests.\n14\t\n15\t**What is broken about how they do it now?**\n16\tContent is scattered across social media accounts or buried on each school's news\n17\tpage. There is no single place, so alumni connect with their own classmates but\n18\tnot with the wider network.\n19\t\n20\t**What would make them say \"this is better\"?**\n21\tOne place where each school's posts and the alumni network live together, so the\n22\tnetwork connects people across schools and not only within one.\n23\t\n24\t**What must not happen?**\n25\t- An editor at one school must never delete another school's pages.\n26\t- An editor must not approve or deny alumni for any school but their own. Once a\n27\t  Fordham Prep alumnus is approved by Fordham Prep, a school like Xavier High\n28\t  School (New York) must still grant that person access to see its posts and\n29\t  alumni network.\n30\t- Contact details are private by default and shared only as the alumnus chooses\n31\t  (see below).\n32\t\n33\t**Alumni privacy (client's wish):** each alumnus can set phone, email and occupation\n34\tto private, visible only to their own school, or visible to all schools in the\n35\tnetwork.\n36\t\n37\t---\n38\t\n39\t## Scope notes (for the grill, not the client's words)\n40\t\n41\tThe client's reality is bigger than the seven required capabilities. Open\n42\tquestions to settle in `/grill-with-docs`:\n43\t\n44\t- **Alumni accounts, approval and per-field privacy** need logins for readers. The\n45\t  public site is static HTML, so there are no reader logins, and the admin console\n46\t  never goes on the public internet. These are likely out of scope or reduced to\n47\t  something the static site can do.\n48\t- **Per-school editors** with edit rights limited to their own school need a\n49\t  school-ownership rule on posts and pages. Kept outside `site/`, made-up data.\n57\t"}]},"uuid":"0b3350c0-137f-48e7-89f4-9de99cf2b748","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:02:13.972Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/notes/client-brief.md","content":"# Client brief: Jesuit Schools Network\n\n**Who runs the site, and how comfortable are they with computers?**\nJennifer LaMaster, director of the Jesuit Schools Network, oversees it. She heads a\nbig organization and has a tech team under her, so she supervises and does not\nwrite content day to day. Each member high school has its own editor, usually the\nhead of alumni relations (Fordham Prep's would be JP Feighery). They write\nblog-style posts about their own school and decide which alumni may join.\n\n**Who reads it, and what are they usually trying to find?**\nAlumni of the Jesuit schools. They want news from their own school and from the\nother schools, and a way to reach alumni of other schools who share a Jesuit\neducation and similar interests.\n\n**What is broken about how they do it now?**\nContent is scattered across social media accounts or buried on each school's news\npage. There is no single place, so alumni connect with their own classmates but\nnot with the wider network.\n\n**What would make them say \"this is better\"?**\nOne place where each school's posts and the alumni network live together, so the\nnetwork connects people across schools and not only within one.\n\n**What must not happen?**\n- An editor at one school must never delete another school's pages.\n- An editor must not approve or deny alumni for any school but their own. Once a\n  Fordham Prep alumnus is approved by Fordham Prep, a school like Xavier High\n  School (New York) must still grant that person access to see its posts and\n  alumni network.\n- Contact details are private by default and shared only as the alumnus chooses\n  (see below).\n\n**Alumni privacy (client's wish):** each alumnus can set phone, email and occupation\nto private, visible only to their own school, or visible to all schools in the\nnetwork.\n\n---\n\n## Scope notes (for the grill, not the client's words)\n\nThe client's reality is bigger than the seven required capabilities. Open\nquestions to settle in `/grill-with-docs`:\n\n- **Alumni accounts, approval and per-field privacy** need logins for readers. The\n  public site is static HTML, so there are no reader logins, and the admin console\n  never goes on the public internet. These are likely out of scope or reduced to\n  something the static site can do.\n- **Per-school editors** with edit rights limited to their own school need a\n  school-ownership rule on posts and pages. The required roles are only admin and\n  editor, so \"editor of one school\" must be an editor whose permissions are scoped\n  by school.\n- Decided in-scope version: Jennifer is the admin, school editors are editors\n  scoped to their school, and the public site shows each school's published posts\n  and pages.\n- Stretch, only after the CMS works: a non-functional mockup of the social-network\n  version (alumni directory, privacy settings). {"parentUuid":"0be6248f-d16a-44d8-aa92-5883db2dca43","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"3f2a930c-077d-46f2-b946-e18c7e46eaf9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TmkydCgs2jZ4RxX1LUr8pN","type":"tool_result","content":"ls: cannot access 'GLOSSARY.md': No such file or directory\nCONTEXT.md\ncat: docs/process/CONTEXT.md: No such file or directory\n# Domain Docs\n\nHow the engineering skills should consume this repo's domain documentation when exploring the codebase.\n\n## Before exploring, read these\n\n- **`CONTEXT.md`** at the repo root, or\n- **`CONTEXT-MAP.md`** at the repo root if it exists: it points at one `CONTEXT.md` per context. Read each one relevant to the topic.\n- **`docs/adr/`**: read ADRs that touch the area you're about to work in. In multi-context repos, also check `src/<context>/docs/adr/` for context-scoped decisions.\n\nIf any of these files don't exist, **proceed silently**. Don't flag their absence; don't suggest creating them upfront. The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.\n\n## File structure\n\nSingle-context repo (most repos):\n\n```\n/\n├── CONTEXT.md\n├── docs/adr/\n│   ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n│   └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n└── src/\n```\n\nMulti-context repo (presence of `CONTEXT-MAP.md` at the root):\n\n```\n/\n├── CONTEXT-MAP.md\n├── docs/adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\n└── src/\n    ├── ordering/\n    │   ├── CONTEXT.md\n    │   └── docs/adr/                  ← context-specific decisions\n    └── billing/\n        ├── CONTEXT.md\n        └── docs/adr/\n```\n\n## Use the glossary's vocabulary\n\nWhen your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `CONTEXT.md`. Don't drift to synonyms the glossary explicitly avoids.\n\nIf the concept you need isn't in the glossary yet, that's a signal: either you're inventing language the project doesn't use (reconsider) or there's a real gap (note it for `/domain-modeling`).\n\n## Flag ADR conflicts\n\nIf your output contradicts an existing ADR, surface it explicitly rather than silently overriding:\n\n> _Contradicts ADR-0007 (event-sourced orders), but worth reopening because…_\n## Part 0 — Overview\n\n### 0.1 Why a CMS\n\nEvery newspaper site, club page, and university department runs on one. A CMS is where three ideas meet that you have not built before:\n\n- **Identity and permission.** Who are you, and what are you allowed to do? An editor writes; only an admin creates users. That difference is enforced in code, or it is not real.\n- **Content as data.** A post is not a file of HTML. It is a row with a title, a slug, a body, a status, an author, and dates. Everything else — the admin list, the public page, the navigation — is generated from those rows.\n- **Draft versus published.** The thing you are editing and the thing the world sees are deliberately not the same.\n\nThis is also the first project in this course where a mistake has consequences beyond ugliness: an access-control bug means a user can do something they should not.\n\n### 0.2 The shape of what you build\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n    subgraph laptop[\"YOUR LAPTOP (private)\"]\n        A[\"Admin console<br/>FastAPI + Jinja templates\"] --> DB[(\"SQLite database<br/>users, posts, pages\")]\n        DB --> P[\"cms publish<br/>renders published rows\"]\n    end\n    P --> S[\"site/ — plain HTML\"]\n    S --> GH[\"gh-pages branch\"]\n    GH --> W[\"https://you.github.io/iphs400-mp2-cms/<br/>(public)\"]\n```\n\nThe admin console never goes on the internet. Nobody can attack a server that is not running, and your database and passwords stay on your machine. Publishing is a one-way door: rows in, static HTML out.\n\n### 0.3 The workflow you are learning\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart TD\n    R[\"/research<br/>learn the domain\"] --> G[\"/grill-with-docs<br/>interviewed one question at a time<br/>→ CONTEXT.md + ADRs\"]\n    G --> S[\"/to-spec<br/>→ one spec issue\"]\n    S --> T[\"/to-tickets<br/>→ 7-9 ticket issues,<br/>each a vertical slice\"]\n    T --> I[\"/implement #N<br/>drives /tdd: red → green → refactor\"]\n    I --> C[\"/code-review<br/>fresh subagent reviews the diff\"]\n    C --> X[\"findings posted on the issue,<br/>then commit 'Closes #N'\"]\n    X -->|\"/clear, next ticket\"| I\n```\n\nFour rules make this work, and all four are graded:\n\n1. **The spec is the single source of truth.** When code and spec disagree, one of them is wrong, and you decide which — you do not let it slide.\n2. **`CONTEXT.md` is the shared vocabulary.** If you call it a \"bulletin\" and the model calls it a \"post\", you will get two designs.\n3. **One ticket per fresh context.** `/clear` between tickets. Long tickets get `/compact` at a green test.\n4. **The reviewer must not be the author.** `/code-review` runs in a fresh subagent because an agent that just wrote code approves its own work.\n\n### 0.4 What is given, what you build\n\n| The template gives you | You build |\n|---|---|\n| Stack and dependencies: FastAPI, Jinja2, SQLite, argon2 hashing, Markdown sanitizer | All 7 capabilities |\n| **T00, already done:** a \"hello admin\" page, one passing test, `cms publish` shipping a placeholder to Pages | Your data model, login, roles |\n| Folder layout and test helpers such as `client_as(\"editor\")` | Every route, form, and screen |\n| Two example ADRs, a security checklist, `CLAUDE.md`, hooks, pinned skills, `check_submission.py`, README skeleton | Your own ADRs, spec, tickets, tests |\n\nThe template contains **no CMS features**. That is deliberate: features are what you are being taught to produce.\n\n---\n\n==> docs/adr/ADR-001-local-admin-static-public.md <==\n# ADR-001: The admin console runs locally; the public site is a static export\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nA CMS needs a server, a database, and login. GitHub Pages serves static files\nonly: it cannot run Python and has no database. Students have two weeks and no\nhosting budget, and a first web application with real authentication is an\nattractive target the moment it is public.\n\n## Decision\n\nThe admin console (FastAPI + SQLite) runs on the student's own machine. A\n`cms publish` step renders the published content to static HTML in `site/`, which\nis deployed to GitHub Pages on the `gh-pages` branch.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Login, roles, and the database are never exposed to the internet.\n- The public site cannot have server-side features (search, comments) without a\n  different host; a stretch goal may add a live admin deploy behind a security gate.\n- Publishing is explicit: content changes are not live until `cms publish` runs,\n  which is a behaviour worth documenting for your client.\n- Relative paths become mandatory, because Pages serves the site from a subfolder.\n\n## Why this is an ADR\n\nIt is hard to reverse (it shapes every route, template, and deploy step) and it\nis surprising without context (most CMSes are servers). That is the bar; most\ndecisions do not meet it and belong in the spec or the glossary instead.\n\n==> docs/adr/ADR-002-stack.md <==\n# ADR-002: FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite, with no frontend framework\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nThe project has to be readable by someone who has never built a web application,\ngradable from a clean clone, and finishable in two weeks on a $20 plan. Options\nconsidered: Django (batteries included, but a large amount of generated code to\nread), Flask (similar to FastAPI here), a JavaScript stack (a second language and\na build step), and FastAPI with server-rendered templates.\n\n## Decision\n\nFastAPI with Jinja2 templates, SQLite for storage, argon2 for password hashing,\nand an HTML sanitizer for user Markdown. No frontend framework, no build step.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Every page is server-rendered, so \"view source\" shows what the code produced,\n  which makes debugging visible.\n- SQLite is a single file, so a grader can seed and inspect it easily. It is\n  gitignored; `scripts/seed_demo.py` rebuilds it.\n- No build step means the file you preview is the file that ships.\n- Interactive flourishes (live preview without reload, drag-and-drop) cost more\n  here than in a JavaScript stack. That is an accepted trade.\nls: cannot access 'src': No such file or directory\nls: cannot access 'cms': No such file or directory\napp:\n__init__.py\n__pycache__\ncli.py\nmain.py\npublish.py\nsettings.py\n[project]\nname = \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"96fc6126-f3ba-4c49-866f-38c39fbae144","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:02:17.081Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"ls: cannot access 'GLOSSARY.md': No such file or directory\nCONTEXT.md\ncat: docs/process/CONTEXT.md: No such file or directory\n# Domain Docs\n\nHow the engineering skills should consume this repo's domain documentation when exploring the codebase.\n\n## Before exploring, read these\n\n- **`CONTEXT.md`** at the repo root, or\n- **`CONTEXT-MAP.md`** at the repo root if it exists: it points at one `CONTEXT.md` per context. A CMS is where three ideas meet that you have not built before:\n\n- **Identity and permission.** Who are you, and what are you allowed to do? An editor writes; only an admin creates users. That difference is enforced in code, or it is not real.\n- **Content as data.** A post is not a file of HTML. It is a row with a title, a slug, a body, a status, an author, and dates. Everything else — the admin list, the public page, the navigation — is generated from those rows.\n- **Draft versus published.** The thing you are editing and the thing the world sees are deliberately not the same.\n\nThis is also the first project in this course where a mistake has consequences beyond ugliness: an access-control bug means a user can do something they should not.\n\n### 0.2 The shape of what you build\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n    subgraph laptop[\"YOUR LAPTOP (private)\"]\n        A[\"Admin console<br/>FastAPI + Jinja templates\"] --> DB[(\"SQLite database<br/>users, posts, pages\")]\n        DB --> P[\"cms publish<br/>renders published rows\"]\n    end\n    P --> S[\"site/ — plain HTML\"]\n    S --> GH[\"gh-pages branch\"]\n    GH --> W[\"https://you.github.io/iphs400-mp2-cms/<br/>(public)\"]\n```\n\nThe admin console never goes on the internet. Nobody can attack a server that is not running, and your database and passwords stay on your machine. Publishing is a one-way door: rows in, static HTML out.\n\n### 0.3 The workflow you are learning\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart TD\n    R[\"/research<br/>learn the domain\"] --> G[\"/grill-with-docs<br/>interviewed one question at a time<br/>→ CONTEXT.md + ADRs\"]\n    G --> S[\"/to-spec<br/>→ one spec issue\"]\n    S --> T[\"/to-tickets<br/>→ 7-9 ticket issues,<br/>each a vertical slice\"]\n    T --> I[\"/implement #N<br/>drives /tdd: red → green → refactor\"]\n    I --> C[\"/code-review<br/>fresh subagent reviews the diff\"]\n    C --> X[\"findings posted on the issue,<br/>then commit 'Closes #N'\"]\n    X -->|\"/clear, next ticket\"| I\n```\n\nFour rules make this work, and all four are graded:\n\n1. **The spec is the single source of truth.** When code and spec disagree, one of them is wrong, and you decide which — you do not let it slide.\n2. **`CONTEXT.md` is the shared vocabulary.** If you call it a \"bulletin\" and the model calls it a \"post\", you will get two designs.\n3. **One ticket per fresh context.** `/clear` between tickets. Long tickets get `/compact` at a green test.\n4. **The reviewer must not be the author.** `/code-review` runs in a fresh subagent because an agent that just wrote code approves its own work.\n\n### 0.4 What is given, what you build\n\n| The template gives you | You build |\n|---|---|\n| Stack and dependencies: FastAPI, Jinja2, SQLite, argon2 hashing, Markdown sanitizer | All 7 capabilities |\n| **T00, already done:** a \"hello admin\" page, one passing test, `cms publish` shipping a placeholder to Pages | Your data model, login, roles |\n| Folder layout and test helpers such as `client_as(\"editor\")` | Every route, form, and screen |\n| Two example ADRs, a security checklist, `CLAUDE.md`, hooks, pinned skills, `check_submission.py`, README skeleton | Your own ADRs, spec, tickets, tests |\n\nThe template contains **no CMS features**. That is deliberate: features are what you are being taught to produce.\n\n---\n\n==> docs/adr/ADR-001-local-admin-static-public.md <==\n# ADR-001: The admin console runs locally; the public site is a static export\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nA CMS needs a server, a database, and login. GitHub Pages serves static files\nonly: it cannot run Python and has no database. Students have two weeks and no\nhosting budget, and a first web application with real authentication is an\nattractive target the moment it is public.\n\n## Decision\n\nThe admin console (FastAPI + SQLite) runs on the student's own machine. A\n`cms publish` step renders the published content to static HTML in `site/`, which\nis deployed to GitHub Pages on the `gh-pages` branch.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Login, roles, and the database are never exposed to the internet.\n- The public site cannot have server-side features (search, comments) without a\n  different host; a stretch goal may add a live admin deploy behind a security gate.\n- Publishing is explicit: content changes are not live until `cms publish` runs,\n  which is a behaviour worth documenting for your client.\n- Relative paths become mandatory, because Pages serves the site from a subfolder.\n\n## Why this is an ADR\n\nIt is hard to reverse (it shapes every route, template, and deploy step) and it\nis surprising without context (most CMSes are servers). That is the bar; most\ndecisions do not meet it and belong in the spec or the glossary instead.\n\n==> docs/adr/ADR-002-stack.md <==\n# ADR-002: FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite, with no frontend framework\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nThe project has to be readable by someone who has never built a web application,\ngradable from a clean clone, and finishable in two weeks on a $20 plan. Options\nconsidered: Django (batteries included, but a large amount of generated code to\nread), Flask (similar to FastAPI here), a JavaScript stack (a second language and\na build step), and FastAPI with server-rendered templates.\n\n## Decision\n\nFastAPI with Jinja2 templates, SQLite for storage, argon2 for password hashing,\nand an HTML sanitizer for user Markdown. No frontend framework, no build step.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Every page is server-rendered, so \"view source\" shows what the code produced,\n  which makes debugging visible.\n- SQLite is a single file, so a grader can seed and inspect it easily. It is\n  gitignored; `scripts/seed_demo.py` rebuilds it.\n- No build step means the file you preview is the file that ships.\n- Interactive flourishes (live preview without reload, drag-and-drop) cost more\n  here than in a JavaScript stack. That is an accepted trade.\nls: cannot access 'src': No such file or directory\nls: cannot access 'cms': No such file or directory\napp:\n__init__.py\n__pycache__\ncli.py\nmain.py\npublish.py\nsettings.py\n[project]\nname = \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"08850cc7-c2f4-47b7-86ef-a4c8f41b325a","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":"main","status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":42},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"wsl"}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0be6248f-d16a-44d8-aa92-5883db2dca43","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"bf94b55b-35db-4e0c-b7be-714c29bd5095","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"3f2a930c-077d-46f2-b946-e18c7e46eaf9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MQYRArnfpJ1rKa1727VguA","type":"tool_result","content":"### 3.3 The required capabilities\n\nYour CMS can name things however your client would. Underneath, all seven of these must work. This list is fixed; how you get there is yours.\n\n1. **Login** — admins and editors sign in and out; passwords are stored hashed, never as text.\n2. **Two roles** — *admin* and *editor*, with genuinely different permissions.\n3. **Posts** — create, read, update, delete; title, slug, Markdown body, draft/published, author, timestamps.\n4. **Pages** — same, and published pages appear in the public navigation.\n5. **Admin console** — dashboard with counts, a content list you can filter, an editor with a preview.\n6. **User management** — admin only: create users, change roles, deactivate; a deactivated user cannot log in.\n7. **Public site and publish** — only published content, Markdown sanitized, nav built from pages, `cms publish` writes `site/` with relative paths.\n\n**Deliberately excluded** (do not build these): multi-site, drag-and-drop page builders, rich-text WYSIWYG editors. They cost far more than they teach.\n\n---","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"075a5e81-c865-4c3a-a0ad-0654491fd624","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:02:21.997Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"### 3.3 The required capabilities\n\nYour CMS can name things however your client would. Underneath, all seven of these must work. This list is fixed; how you get there is yours.\n\n1. **Login** — admins and editors sign in and out; passwords are stored hashed, never as text.\n2. **Two roles** — *admin* and *editor*, with genuinely different permissions.\n3. **Posts** — create, read, update, delete; title, slug, Markdown body, draft/published, author, timestamps.\n4. **Pages** — same, and published pages appear in the public navigation.\n5. **Admin console** — dashboard with counts, a content list you can filter, an editor with a preview.\n6. **User management** — admin only: create users, change roles, deactivate; a deactivated user cannot log in.\n7. **Public site and publish** — only published content, Markdown sanitized, nav built from pages, `cms publish` writes `site/` with relative paths.\n\n**Deliberately excluded** (do not build these): multi-site, drag-and-drop page builders, rich-text WYSIWYG editors. They cost far more than they teach.\n\n---","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"17bc37b9-6b93-49f4-88e2-393fff87462c","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":"main","status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":42},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"joehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"wsl"}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"bf94b55b-35db-4e0c-b7be-714c29bd5095","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":492,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2569,"cache_read_input_tokens":53102,"cache_creation_input_tokens":492,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":492},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"249670d1-9c2d-4051-aa31-163a6e08c7b4","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CfyLM6j9DjqrZM4Q9xnLf","attributionSkill":"domain-modeling","type":"assistant","uuid":"818b90b0-0099-4e92-b638-86aa19fd2e8b","timestamp":"2026-10-13T01:02:36.010Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"818b90b0-0099-4e92-b638-86aa19fd2e8b","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfyLM72kibkpfBbK8SKF4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've read the brief, the field notes, the manual's seven capabilities, the two ADRs, and `app/`. `CONTEXT.md` is an empty stub, so I'll record terms there as they settle. `CLAUDE.md` names it as the glossary, so I'm using it instead of `GLOSSARY.md`.\n\nThe brief already decides the in-scope version: Jennifer is the admin, school editors are scoped by school, and the public site shows each school's published posts and pages. That settles some branches. Round 1 covers what's still open and doesn't depend on your other answers.\n\n❓ **Q1** - **Is \"School\" a thing in the data, and who owns content?**: The brief says edit rights are scoped by school. That needs a School record (name, short name) and every post and page belonging to exactly one School. Jennifer also needs somewhere for network-wide content like \"About the network\" and \"How to join\". Should that belong to a pseudo-school called \"Network\", or to no school at all?\n\n➡️ School is a first-class record. Every post and page has exactly one School, and \"Jesuit Schools Network\" is itself a School row, so the rule is uniform: no nulls, no special cases. Only the admin can edit Network content.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q2** - **What exactly can each role do?**: The manual fixes two roles, admin and editor. I'd define them like this:\n- **Admin** (Jennifer): everything across all schools, plus user management (create, change role, deactivate) and publishing.\n- **Editor**: assigned to exactly one School. They can create, edit and delete posts and pages of their own School only. They can't see other Schools' drafts, manage users, or change their own School.\n\nIs that right? Specifically, should an editor be able to see other schools' drafts in the admin list, or only their own School's?\n\n➡️ Exactly as above. Editors see only their own School's content in the console, drafts included. Other schools' published content is visible on the public site only. That makes \"never delete another school's page\" structural, because the page isn't even in their list, and not just a button hidden in the UI.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q3** - **Alumni: confirm they're out of scope, and what replaces them**: Alumni logins, approval by school and per-field privacy can't exist on a static public site. The admin console is local-only (ADR-001). Phone, email and occupation are the sensitive data. Should the CMS store alumni personal data at all?\n\n➡️ No. The CMS holds no alumni records, so the privacy requirement is met by never collecting contact details. The \"connect across schools\" goal is served by cross-school discovery instead: one site where every School's posts appear together and the Schools are listed. The alumni directory and privacy settings stay in the stretch mockup, outside `site/`, with made-up data.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q4** - **What does the public site look like?**: The core value is \"one place where each school's posts live together\". Options:\n- (a) A single merged feed on the home page (newest first, labelled with School), plus a page per School listing that School's posts.\n- (b) Only per-School sections, no merged feed.\n\nAnd navigation: the manual says published Pages appear in the nav. Should nav be flat (Network pages + a \"Schools\" menu), or should each School's pages nest under that School?\n\n➡️ (a). The merged feed is the \"better than now\" moment for Jennifer. Nav is two-level: Network pages at top, with a Schools entry leading to each School's page, which lists that School's posts and pages. That keeps the nav short as Schools are added.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q5** - **Who publishes, and does publishing cover everything?**: Publishing runs `cms publish` on a machine with the database, which makes it a network-wide step because `site/` is a single export. Editors can't realistically run a CLI. Should publishing be an admin-only act (Jennifer or her tech team), with editors marking posts \"published\" and waiting for the next publish? That means a published post isn't live until the next `cms publish`.\n\n➡️ Yes. Two separate things need two words: a post's **status** (draft or published) is an editor's decision, and **publish** is the admin's network-wide export. Status says \"ready\", publish says \"live\". I'd record the delay in the glossary and the report, not hide it.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q6** - **Slugs and URLs**: Your field notes say WordPress URLs were longer than expected and the permalinks tab was hard to find. Editors like JP won't remember slugs. Should slugs be generated from the title and editable only by the admin? And unique per School, or across the whole site? Per-School uniqueness is fine if URLs include the School (`fordham-prep/spring-gala.html`).\n\n➡️ Auto-generated from the title, editable by the editor but never required. Unique per School within posts, and within pages. URLs are `<school>/<slug>.html`, short and predictable. This is a direct answer to your note #4 (long URLs) and #3 (hidden permalinks).\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q7** - **Deleting**: The brief's worst-case failure is deleting another school's pages. Within their own School, should editors be able to hard-delete a post or page? Or only unpublish it, with deletion reserved for the admin?\n\n➡️ Editors can delete their own School's drafts but can only unpublish (set back to draft) published content. Only the admin can permanently delete published content. Deletion is irreversible and a published page may be linked from elsewhere, so a second person approves.\n\n---\n\nAnswer by number. Override any recommendation and say why, since those overrides are the best evidence of your own judgment. {"parentUuid":"3998aa35-89cf-46e6-8d59-fc16d590d464","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"5d8dd3cb-11ee-4721-9610-cb576942242b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YXdgqeJ7gzqpxY9HC1QbwW","type":"tool_result","content":"# GLOSSARY.md Format\n\n## Structure\n\n```md\n# {Context Name}\n\n{One or two sentence description of what this context is and why it exists.}\n\n## Language\n\n**Order**:\n{A one or two sentence description of the term}\n_Avoid_: Purchase, transaction\n\n**Invoice**:\nA request for payment sent to a customer after delivery.\n_Avoid_: Bill, payment request\n\n**Customer**:\nA person or organization that places orders.\n_Avoid_: Client, buyer, account\n```\n\n## Rules\n\n- **Be opinionated.** When multiple words exist for the same concept, pick the best one and list the others under `_Avoid_`.\n- **Keep definitions tight.** One or two sentences max. Define what it IS, not what it does.\n- **Only include terms specific to this project's context.** General programming concepts (timeouts, error types, utility patterns) don't belong even if the project uses them extensively. Before adding a term, ask: is this a concept unique to this context, or a general programming concept? Only the former belongs.\n- **Group terms under subheadings** when natural clusters emerge. If all terms belong to a single cohesive area, a flat list is fine.\n\n## Single vs multi-context repos\n\n**Single context (most repos):** One `GLOSSARY.md` at the repo root.\n\n**Multiple contexts:** A `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` at the repo root lists the contexts, where they live, and how they relate to each other:\n\n```md\n# Glossary Map\n\n## Contexts\n\n- [Ordering](./src/ordering/GLOSSARY.md): receives and tracks customer orders\n- [Billing](./src/billing/GLOSSARY.md): generates invoices and processes payments\n- [Fulfillment](./src/fulfillment/GLOSSARY.md): manages warehouse picking and shipping\n\n## Relationships\n\n- **Ordering → Fulfillment**: Ordering emits `OrderPlaced` events; Fulfillment consumes them to start picking\n- **Fulfillment → Billing**: Fulfillment emits `ShipmentDispatched` events; Billing consumes them to generate invoices\n- **Ordering ↔ Billing**: Shared types for `CustomerId` and `Money`\n```\n\nThe skill infers which structure applies:\n\n- If `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` exists, read it to find contexts\n- If only a root `GLOSSARY.md` exists, single context\n- If neither exists, create a root `GLOSSARY.md` lazily when the first term is resolved\n\nWhen multiple contexts exist, infer which one the current topic relates to. Define what it IS, not what it does.\n- **Only include terms specific to this project's context.** General programming concepts (timeouts, error types, utility patterns) don't belong even if the project uses them extensively. Before adding a term, ask: is this a concept unique to this context, or a general programming concept? Only the former belongs.\n- **Group terms under subheadings** when natural clusters emerge. If all terms belong to a single cohesive area, a flat list is fine.\n\n## Single vs multi-context repos\n\n**Single context (most repos):** One `GLOSSARY.md` at the repo root.\n\n**Multiple contexts:** A `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` at the repo root lists the contexts, where they live, and how they relate to each other:\n\n```md\n# Glossary Map\n\n## Contexts\n\n- [Ordering](./src/ordering/GLOSSARY.md): receives and tracks customer orders\n- [Billing](./src/billing/GLOSSARY.md): generates invoices and processes payments\n- [Fulfillment](./src/fulfillment/GLOSSARY.md): manages warehouse picking and shipping\n\n## Relationships\n\n- **Ordering → Fulfillment**: Ordering emits `OrderPlaced` events; Fulfillment consumes them to start picking\n- **Fulfillment → Billing**: Fulfillment emits `ShipmentDispatched` events; Billing consumes them to generate invoices\n- **Ordering ↔ Billing**: Shared types for `CustomerId` and `Money`\n```\n\nThe skill infers which structure applies:\n\n- If `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` exists, read it to find contexts\n- If only a root `GLOSSARY.md` exists, single context\n- If neither exists, create a root `GLOSSARY.md` lazily when the first term is resolved\n\nWhen multiple contexts exist, infer which one the current topic relates to. {"parentUuid":"db6c72bf-658e-46f6-886f-2fee8d4eacea","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cg2z2mLWTEogG2vk3tYry","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01CXww2174SASWAb9z39wbYH","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/CONTEXT.md","content":"# Jesuit Schools Network CMS\n\nA content system for the Jesuit Schools Network. Each member school's editor writes about their own school; the network director oversees everything; alumni read the result on one public site.\n\n## Language\n\n**Network**:\nThe Jesuit Schools Network as a whole, run by its director.\n_Avoid_: Organization, association\n\n**School**:\nOne of the roughly 60 member high schools. The set is fixed and entered by the Admin; every Post and Page belongs to exactly one School.\n_Avoid_: Site, tenant, campus\n\n**Admin**:\nThe Network director (Jennifer LaMaster), who may act on every School's content and manages Users.\n_Avoid_: Superuser, owner\n\n**Editor**:\nA User assigned to exactly one School who writes and manages that School's Posts and Pages, and nothing else.\n_Avoid_: Author, school admin\n\n**Post**:\nA dated, blog-style entry about a School, shown in the merged news feed.\n_Avoid_: Article, story, news item\n\n**Page**:\nA standing piece of content (not dated news) that appears in the public navigation once published.\n_Avoid_: Section\n\n**Alumnus**:\nA reader of the public site. The CMS holds no records about Alumni.\n_Avoid_: Member, user, subscriber\n\n## Relationships\n\n- A **School** has many **Editors**, **Posts** and **Pages**; each Editor, Post and Page has exactly one **School**.\n- An **Editor** sees and changes only their own **School**'s content, drafts included.\n- Only the **Admin** permanently deletes published content; an **Editor** may delete their School's drafts and unpublish published content.\n\n## Flagged ambiguities\n\n- Who runs **publish**, and whether network-wide content (About, How to join) belongs to a School or to the Network itself, are still being settled.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1374,"cache_read_input_tokens":56368,"output_tokens":727,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1374,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":727,"cache_read_input_tokens":56368,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1374,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1374},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01CXww2174SASWAb9z39wbYH":{"file_path":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/CONTEXT.md","content":"# Jesuit Schools Network CMS\n\nA content system for the Jesuit Schools Network. {"parentUuid":"8a5aa19d-eb2f-43a2-9eaf-047c9957dea7","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cg2z3DiGbQHHeLJh2DvMx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Q2, Q3, Q6 and Q7 are settled, and `CONTEXT.md` now holds School, Admin, Editor, Post, Page, Alumnus and the main relationships. Three points need more from you before I can build on them.\n\n❓ **Q5 (reopened)** - **Who triggers publish?**: Your answer reads two ways. \"Publish by the editor\" would mean each editor runs the export. \"Yes your recommendation\" means the Admin runs it and editors only set a post to published. They conflict, and ADR-001 constrains the choice: the console and database live on one local machine, and `cms publish` writes a single `site/` for all 60 schools. An editor can't run it from their own laptop without sharing the database. So which do you mean?\n- (a) **Admin-only publish.** Editors set a post's status to \"published\"; Jennifer (or her tech team) runs `cms publish`, and it goes live then.\n- (b) **Editors trigger publish.** This works only if the console has a \"Publish\" button that any editor can press, and it rebuilds the whole network's site.\n\n➡️ (a). It matches ADR-001 and keeps one person responsible for what goes live, and a network-wide rebuild by any of 60 editors raises the risk of publishing someone else's half-finished work. Because the console is local, (b) would have to be demonstrated as one machine with several logins anyway. If what you want is for editors to feel in control of going live, that is a UI question we can settle later: a \"Ready to publish\" indicator on each post.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q1b** - **Schools: how do they get into the system?**: You said there are about 60 Schools, the set never changes, and Jennifer lists them. I read that as a School management screen being unnecessary. Two ways to load them:\n- (a) A seed file with all 60 (name, short name, city/state) loaded once. The Admin never adds or removes a School in the console.\n- (b) An admin-only screen to add and edit Schools, which is overkill if the list never changes.\n\nAlso, where does network-wide content (About, How to join) belong? In your wording every School is \"its own input\", so I need to know whether Network content is outside that.\n\n➡️ (a) for Schools. Add a \"Jesuit Schools Network\" entry as a 61st School row that only the Admin can write to. That keeps the rule \"every post and page has one School\" with no exceptions. The seed carries city and state, because Q4's map mockup needs a location for each School. I'd store the 60 in a CSV and load it with the demo seed (`scripts/seed_demo.py` already rebuilds the database).\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q4b** - **The map: how far does it go?**: An interactive US map is a good idea, but the real site is static HTML with no JavaScript framework (ADR-002) and the required capabilities don't include it. Should it be:\n- (a) A mockup only, outside `site/`, like the alumni-directory stretch: made-up data, not generated by `cms publish`.\n- (b) Part of the generated site: the Schools index page gets a map built from each School's city/state, with a plain list as the fallback.\n\nWhichever you choose, the Schools index page must list all 60 Schools as a plain text list, because 60 entries in the navigation is unusable and a map alone is not accessible.\n\n➡️ (a), after the CMS works, as you described. The map needs coordinates, an SVG of the US, and click handling, which is a lot of work for something outside the seven required capabilities. The alphabetical list stays the real navigation. Keeping the city/state fields in the seed (Q1b) means the mockup can be promoted into `site/` later without a schema change.\n\n---\n\nAfter these, the next round is the dependent branches: how Users are created and assigned a School, deactivation, the draft preview, the dashboard counts, and the out-of-scope list."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":811,"cache_read_input_tokens":57742,"output_tokens":1244,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":811,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1244,"cache_read_input_tokens":57742,"cache_creation_input_tokens":811,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":811},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"6c8f1e5b-9cb0-4a13-a1a5-a57ca2a2d199","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011Cg2z3DSQbVLR9fP3DF3Zh","type":"assistant","uuid":"62a96bc6-ec3b-433d-8a9d-aa4f3c96350d","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:18:37.981Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

It owns network-wide Pages (About, How to join); only the Admin writes to it.\n_Avoid_: Global content, site-wide page\n\n**Admin**:","originalFile":"# Jesuit Schools Network CMS\n\nA content system for the Jesuit Schools Network. Each member school's editor writes about their own school; the network director oversees everything; alumni read the result on one public site.\n\n## Language\n\n**Network**:\nThe Jesuit Schools Network as a whole, run by its director.\n_Avoid_: Organization, association\n\n**School**:\nOne of the roughly 60 member high schools. The set is fixed and entered by the Admin; every Post and Page belongs to exactly one School.\n_Avoid_: Site, tenant, campus\n\n**Admin**:\nThe Network director (Jennifer LaMaster), who may act on every School's content and manages Users.\n_Avoid_: Superuser, owner\n\n**Editor**:\nA User assigned to exactly one School who writes and manages that School's Posts and Pages, and nothing else.\n_Avoid_: Author, school admin\n\n**Post**:\nA dated, blog-style entry about a School, shown in the merged news feed.\n_Avoid_: Article, story, news item\n\n**Page**:\nA standing piece of content (not dated news) that appears in the public navigation once published.\n_Avoid_: Section\n\n**Alumnus**:\nA reader of the public site. The CMS holds no records about Alumni.\n_Avoid_: Member, user, subscriber\n\n## Relationships\n\n- A **School** has many **Editors**, **Posts** and **Pages**; each Editor, Post and Page has exactly one **School**.\n- An **Editor** sees and changes only their own **School**'s content, drafts included.\n- Only the **Admin** permanently deletes published content; an **Editor** may delete their School's drafts and unpublish published content.\n\n## Flagged ambiguities\n\n- Who runs **publish**, and whether network-wide content (About, How to join) belongs to a School or to the Network itself, are still being settled.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":12,"oldLines":6,"newStart":12,"newLines":10,"lines":[" One of the roughly 60 member high schools. The set is fixed and entered by the Admin; every Post and Page belongs to exactly one School.\n_Avoid_: Site, tenant, campus\n\n**Network School**:\nThe one School row that stands for the Network itself. It owns network-wide Pages (About, How to join); only the Admin writes to it.\n_Avoid_: Global content, site-wide page\n\n**Admin**:\nThe Network director (Jennifer LaMaster), who may act on every School's content and manages Users.\n_Avoid_: Superuser, owner\n\n**Editor**:\nA User assigned to exactly one School who writes and manages that School's Posts and Pages, and nothing else.\n_Avoid_: Author, school admin\n\n**Post**:\nA dated, blog-style entry about a School, shown in the merged news feed.\n_Avoid_: Article, story, news item\n\n**Page**:\nA standing piece of content (not dated news) that appears in the public navigation once published.\n_Avoid_: Section\n\n**Alumnus**:\nA reader of the public site.
