{"parentUuid":"24146036-4a8e-4826-8bf6-171a264779cc","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"15355167-caba-4c74-8b8c-1732576aae01","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0147JGEx8DcFtWHum9jZYR9E","type":"tool_result","content":"The file /home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/CONTEXT.md has been updated successfully. (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"uuid":"e16908ec-5e28-4e85-8f39-9a1a558dd080","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:20:48.329Z","toolUseResult":{"filePath":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/CONTEXT.md","oldString":"- Who runs **publish**, and whether network-wide content (About, How to join) belongs to a School or to the Network itself, are still being settled.\n","newString":"- None open right now.\n","originalFile":"# Jesuit Schools Network CMS\n\nA content system for the Jesuit Schools Network. Each member school's editor writes about their own school; the network director oversees everything; alumni read the result on one public site.\n\n## Language\n\n**Network**:\nThe Jesuit Schools Network as a whole, run by its director.\n_Avoid_: Organization, association\n\n**School**:\nOne of the roughly 60 member high schools. The set is fixed and entered by the Admin; every Post and Page belongs to exactly one School.\n_Avoid_: Site, tenant, campus\n\n**Network School**:\nThe one School row that stands for the Network itself. It owns network-wide Pages (About, How to join); only the Admin writes to it.\n_Avoid_: Global content, site-wide page\n\n**Admin**:\nThe Network director (Jennifer LaMaster), who may act on every School's content and manages Users.\n_Avoid_: Superuser, owner\n\n**Editor**:\nA User assigned to exactly one School who writes and manages that School's Posts and Pages, and nothing else.\n_Avoid_: Author, school admin\n\n**Post**:\nA dated, blog-style entry about a School, shown in the merged news feed.\n_Avoid_: Article, story, news item\n\n**Page**:\nA standing piece of content (not dated news) that appears in the public navigation once published.\n_Avoid_: Section\n\n**Status**:\nWhether a Post or Page is a draft or published. An Editor decides it; it says \"ready\", not \"live\".\n_Avoid_: State, visibility\n\n**Publish**:\nThe Admin's act of exporting every published Post and Page, for all Schools at once, to the public site. A published item goes live only at the next Publish.\n_Avoid_: Deploy, release, go live\n\n**Alumnus**:\nA reader of the public site. The CMS holds no records about Alumni.\n_Avoid_: Member, user, subscriber\n\n## Relationships\n\n- A **School** has many **Editors**, **Posts** and **Pages**; each Editor, Post and Page has exactly one **School**.\n- An **Editor** sees and changes only their own **School**'s content, drafts included.\n- Only the **Admin** permanently deletes published content; an **Editor** may delete their School's drafts and unpublish published content.\n\n## Flagged ambiguities\n\n- Who runs **publish**, and whether network-wide content (About, How to join) belongs to a School or to the Network itself, are still being settled.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":52,"oldLines":4,"newStart":52,"newLines":4,"lines":[" "," ## Flagged ambiguities"," ","-- Who runs **publish**, and whether network-wide content (About, How to join) belongs to a School or to the Network itself, are still being settled.","+- None open right now."]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"24146036-4a8e-4826-8bf6-171a264779cc","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"e16908ec-5e28-4e85-8f39-9a1a558dd080","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"total_tokens_reminder","text":"<total_tokens>14998693 tokens left</total_tokens>"},"type":"attachment","uuid":"664ca427-0c1a-483e-8ec3-578e3c9efb21","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:20:48.333Z","rendered":[{"content":"<system-reminder>\n<total_tokens>14998693 tokens left</total_tokens>\n</system-reminder>"}],"session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"664ca427-0c1a-483e-8ec3-578e3c9efb21","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"deferred_tools_record","entries":[],"toolInputCopies":[{"id":"toolu_01KXWX5mJR8qTKhuHJiLjXpn","copy":"wire"},{"id":"toolu_01GhCDDtKFRnzLsh54HzwrHQ","copy":"wire"},{"id":"toolu_0147JGEx8DcFtWHum9jZYR9E","copy":"wire"}]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"2305fd12-bd8a-4fed-94ce-884eb17abef3","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:21:02.068Z","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"2305fd12-bd8a-4fed-94ce-884eb17abef3","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cg2zDiDoNQtLuwZeRAcG5","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Q1, Q4 and Q5 are settled:\n- **Q1:** the Schools come from a seed file, so there's no School management screen.\n- **Q4:** the map is a mockup outside `site/`.\n- **Q5:** only the Admin publishes.\n\nI took your \"A\" on Q1 to include my suggestion of a Network School row for network-wide pages, since you didn't object to it. I added **Network School**, **Status** and **Publish** to `CONTEXT.md`. Round 3 covers users, editing rules, and scope.\n\n❓ **Q8** - **How do Users get created and given a School?**: Only the Admin creates Users (capability 6). For each Editor the Admin picks a School from the seeded list. The Admin sets the first password, since there's no email to send a reset link. Should a new User be forced to change that password at first login? And can the Admin move an Editor to a different School later, for example when alumni relations staff change?\n\n➡️ Yes to forced change at first login (the Admin knows the temporary password, so it can't stay). Yes, the Admin can reassign an Editor's School. Content stays with its School and does not move with the Editor. Passwords are argon2-hashed and never shown or logged.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q9** - **What happens when an Editor is deactivated?**: A deactivated User can't log in (capability 6). Their existing Posts and Pages stay under the School, so the School's content doesn't vanish when JP retires. Should an active session of a deactivated User also stop working immediately? And does the byline on a Post show the author's name publicly, or only the School?\n\n➡️ Sessions stop immediately, because the cookie is checked against the database on every request. The public byline shows the School only, never the author's name. The author is recorded in the console for accountability, but the public site doesn't need individual staff names, and this fits your privacy wish.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q10** - **Editing a published Post**: With no revision history (it isn't one of the seven capabilities), editing a published Post changes the stored copy straight away. Those edits reach the public site only at the next Publish. So between Publishes the console can hold changes that aren't live yet. Is that acceptable, or should an edit to a published Post put it back to draft?\n\n➡️ Edit in place; it goes live at the next Publish. Reverting to draft on every edit would take a typo fix offline, which is worse. The dashboard should show a count of Posts and Pages changed since the last Publish, so Jennifer knows whether a Publish is needed.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q11** - **What does the dashboard show?**: Capability 5 requires counts. Editors should see counts for their own School. The Admin sees the whole Network. Proposed counts: Posts (draft / published), Pages (draft / published), and \"changed since last Publish\". The Admin also sees a per-School breakdown, so Jennifer can see which of the 60 Schools aren't posting. Is the per-School breakdown useful to her?\n\n➡️ Yes. It is the one place where her supervising role shows up in the console. The content list is filterable by Status and type for everyone, and by School for the Admin only.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q12** - **Page order in the navigation**: Published Pages appear in the nav. Alphabetical order is simplest, but \"About\" and \"How to join\" belong first. Should Pages have a manual order number, or should the Admin pin the Network School's Pages first and everything else go alphabetical?\n\n➡️ Network School Pages first, in an order number set by the Admin. School Pages are alphabetical within their School. Editors never think about ordering, and a single order field on Network Pages is the smallest thing that works.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q13** - **What is out of scope?**: Your last stop condition is naming what we're not doing. I'd list these:\n- Alumni accounts, directory, approval and privacy settings (mockup only)\n- Image uploads and media library (Markdown can link to images hosted elsewhere)\n- Comments, search, tags or categories\n- Scheduled publishing and revision history\n- Rich-text editing and a page builder (excluded by the manual)\n- Email, password reset by email, and any live deployment of the console\n\nAnything on this list that you want in, or anything missing?\n\n➡️ Keep the list as written. The one risk is images: school news posts usually have photos. Allowing a Markdown image link to a trusted external URL costs nothing, but then the sanitizer must keep `<img>`. Relative paths only applies to our own links, so I'd allow external image URLs and say so in the spec.\n\n---\n\nOnce these settle, I think the frontier is nearly empty. The remaining step is a final walkthrough of two or three concrete scenarios against the rules, and then your confirmation before `/to-spec`."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1539,"cache_read_input_tokens":59835,"output_tokens":1548,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1539,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1548,"cache_read_input_tokens":59835,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1539,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1539},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"19456d29-64c6-4e7f-a9fb-cc0afcb1e515","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011Cg2zDhyQgPE2DpTmKyY86","type":"assistant","uuid":"4045c05e-3336-4bb2-9043-ad85fa9a4903","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:21:02.068Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use.\n- anthropic-skills:chrome-browser: Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use.\n- anthropic-skills:computer-use: Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly.\n- anthropic-skills:docs: docs (editable docs people share and comment on; the default for any document, named as a doc or not: a document, report, proposal, resume, cover letter, letter, contract, policy, form, template, worksheet, essay, handbook, guide, how-to, cheat sheet, SOP or other writing to keep, share, collaborate on, send, submit, print or sign; a doc exports to Word, PDF, Markdown or Google Docs, so needing a file to send, attach, upload, submit or print is no reason to pick Word, and a file nobody asked for is a doc, not Word; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat; a pasted claude.ai artifact link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; Word or another file format named, tracked changes wanted, or a .docx to change or use as a template → that format's skill): making one → if no docs-connector instructions are in context, call its `guide` (topic.instructions) first; then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of Microsoft Word Documents, such as 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', 'microsoft doc'. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead, even if they will email or print it. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:google-workspace: Read this before the first Google Drive, Docs, Sheets or Slides connector call whenever the task creates or changes a Google file. Use this skill whenever the user wants to create or change a Google Doc, Sheet or Slides file in their Google Drive. Triggers include: a request that names Google Docs, Sheets, Slides or Drive and asks to make, edit, format, copy or rename a file; a docs.google.com link with a request to change that file, even a one-line fix or suggested edits; and any follow-up change to a Google file from earlier in the chat, even \"change it\" or \"add a tab\". Includes helper scripts for document positions, cell ranges and slide layout. However, if the user asks for a doc, deck or spreadsheet without naming Google, or gives a Google file only as source material for something new, use Claude's own output type instead. Do NOT use for read-only questions about a Google file, or for Word, Excel, PowerPoint or PDF files.\n- anthropic-skills:import-memory: Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly.\n- anthropic-skills:docs: docs (editable docs people share and comment on; the default for any document, named as a doc or not: a document, report, proposal, resume, cover letter, letter, contract, policy, form, template, worksheet, essay, handbook, guide, how-to, cheat sheet, SOP or other writing to keep, share, collaborate on, send, submit, print or sign; a doc exports to Word, PDF, Markdown or Google Docs, so needing a file to send, attach, upload, submit or print is no reason to pick Word, and a file nobody asked for is a doc, not Word; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat; a pasted claude.ai artifact link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; Word or another file format named, tracked changes wanted, or a .docx to change or use as a template → that format's skill): making one → if no docs-connector instructions are in context, call its `guide` (topic.instructions) first; then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of Microsoft Word Documents, such as 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', 'microsoft doc'. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead, even if they will email or print it. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:google-workspace: Read this before the first Google Drive, Docs, Sheets or Slides connector call whenever the task creates or changes a Google file. Use this skill whenever the user wants to create or change a Google Doc, Sheet or Slides file in their Google Drive. Triggers include: a request that names Google Docs, Sheets, Slides or Drive and asks to make, edit, format, copy or rename a file; a docs.google.com link with a request to change that file, even a one-line fix or suggested edits; and any follow-up change to a Google file from earlier in the chat, even \"change it\" or \"add a tab\". Includes helper scripts for document positions, cell ranges and slide layout. However, if the user asks for a doc, deck or spreadsheet without naming Google, or gives a Google file only as source material for something new, use Claude's own output type instead. Do NOT use for read-only questions about a Google file, or for Word, Excel, PowerPoint or PDF files.\n- anthropic-skills:import-memory: Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n</system-reminder>"}],"userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

(file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"uuid":"3be322fe-ca82-476f-9d0b-78a2e0a09a58","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:25:01.910Z","toolUseResult":{"filePath":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/CONTEXT.md","oldString":"**Status**:","newString":"**Pinned Page**:\nA Network School Page the Admin places in a fixed position at the front of the public navigation. School Pages are listed alphabetically after them.\n_Avoid_: Sticky page, featured page\n\n**Deactivated User**:\nA User who can no longer log in, immediately, while everything they wrote stays with its School.\n_Avoid_: Deleted user, banned user\n\n**Status**:","originalFile":"# Jesuit Schools Network CMS\n\nA content system for the Jesuit Schools Network. Each member school's editor writes about their own school; the network director oversees everything; alumni read the result on one public site.\n\n## Language\n\n**Network**:\nThe Jesuit Schools Network as a whole, run by its director.\n_Avoid_: Organization, association\n\n**School**:\nOne of the roughly 60 member high schools. The set is fixed and entered by the Admin; every Post and Page belongs to exactly one School.\n_Avoid_: Site, tenant, campus\n\n**Network School**:\nThe one School row that stands for the Network itself. It owns network-wide Pages (About, How to join); only the Admin writes to it.\n_Avoid_: Global content, site-wide page\n\n**Admin**:\nThe Network director (Jennifer LaMaster), who may act on every School's content and manages Users.\n_Avoid_: Superuser, owner\n\n**Editor**:\nA User assigned to exactly one School who writes and manages that School's Posts and Pages, and nothing else.\n_Avoid_: Author, school admin\n\n**Post**:\nA dated, blog-style entry about a School, shown in the merged news feed.\n_Avoid_: Article, story, news item\n\n**Page**:\nA standing piece of content (not dated news) that appears in the public navigation once published.\n_Avoid_: Section\n\n**Status**:\nWhether a Post or Page is a draft or published. An Editor decides it; it says \"ready\", not \"live\".\n_Avoid_: State, visibility\n\n**Publish**:\nThe Admin's act of exporting every published Post and Page, for all Schools at once, to the public site. A published item goes live only at the next Publish.\n_Avoid_: Deploy, release, go live\n\n**Alumnus**:\nA reader of the public site. The CMS holds no records about Alumni.\n_Avoid_: Member, user, subscriber\n\n## Relationships\n\n- A **School** has many **Editors**, **Posts** and **Pages**; each Editor, Post and Page has exactly one **School**.\n- An **Editor** sees and changes only their own **School**'s content, drafts included.\n- Only the **Admin** permanently deletes published content; an **Editor** may delete their School's drafts and unpublish published content.\n\n## Flagged ambiguities\n\n- None open right now.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":32,"oldLines":6,"newStart":32,"newLines":14,"lines":[" A standing piece of content (not dated news) that appears in the public navigation once published."," _Avoid_: Section"," ","+**Pinned Page**:","+A Network School Page the Admin places in a fixed position at the front of the public navigation. School Pages are listed alphabetically after them.","+_Avoid_: Sticky page, featured page","+","+**Deactivated User**:","+A User who can no longer log in, immediately, while everything they wrote stays with its School.","+_Avoid_: Deleted user, banned user","+"," **Status**:"," Whether a Post or Page is a draft or published. An Editor decides it; it says \"ready\", not \"live\"."," _Avoid_: State, visibility"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0ebcbb6e-b85b-477b-8d34-e682cf3a0449","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"bc8d756a-bfaa-4c1d-8ac0-8a32535f7351","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"deferred_tools_record","entries":[],"toolInputCopies":[{"id":"toolu_01TGwJaVzPjNr19byv1R68HK","copy":"wire"}]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"f422e91d-83c9-4365-a1a6-631e13cd321e","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:25:12.569Z","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"f422e91d-83c9-4365-a1a6-631e13cd321e","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cg2zYPJnVmXHx1U21uG9K","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Q8 to Q13 are settled. I added **Pinned Page** and **Deactivated User** to `CONTEXT.md`.\n\nThese are the decisions that now hold:\n- **Users:** the Admin creates Users, assigns an Editor's School, sets a temporary password that must be changed at first login, and can reassign the School.\n- **Deactivation:** takes effect immediately.\n- **Bylines:** public posts show the School only.\n- **Editing:** a published Post is edited in place and goes live at the next Publish.\n- **Dashboard:** counts include \"changed since last Publish\" and a per-School breakdown for the Admin.\n- **Navigation:** Pinned Pages come first.\n- **Out of scope:** the list as written, with external image links allowed.\n\nThe last step is a short stress test of the rules against concrete scenarios, so we don't leave gaps in the spec. Three of them need a decision.\n\n❓ **Q14** - **An Editor probes another School's content**: An Xavier Editor changes the URL to `/posts/17/edit` (a Fordham Prep Post) in the console. What should they see? A \"forbidden\" message tells them the Post exists. \"Not found\" says nothing.\n\n➡️ \"Not found\" (404), for edit, delete and status-change routes alike. The rule is enforced in the data query, not the template: an Editor's queries are filtered to their School, so another School's rows are not found at all. Tests should cover edit, delete and unpublish across Schools for each route.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q15** - **Unpublishing and disappearing content**: JP unpublishes a Fordham Prep Post that alumni have linked to. At the next Publish its page is gone from `site/`, and the old link breaks (a 404 on GitHub Pages). Should Publish rebuild `site/` from scratch each time, so unpublished or deleted items always disappear? Or keep old pages?\n\n➡️ Rebuild from scratch each time. That's the only way to satisfy \"drafts never leave the database\". Leaving a stale file would keep an unpublished post online. The broken-link cost is acceptable and belongs in the glossary and the report. (It also answers the manual's rule that `site/` is generated, never hand-edited.)\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q16** - **Reserved and colliding slugs**: Public URLs are `<school>/<slug>.html`. A Page slugged `index` would overwrite a School's listing page, and the Network home needs a few fixed paths of its own (the Schools index, the feed). Should the CMS reject reserved slugs (`index` and the fixed names), and tell the Editor in plain words when one is taken?\n\n➡️ Yes: a short reserved list, rejected with a plain-language message such as \"That address is already used by the site; please choose another\". On a duplicate slug within a School, the form suggests the next free one (`spring-gala-2`) instead of showing an error, as JP will never remember the rules.\n\n---\n\nThat should empty the frontier: School, roles, Status versus Publish, the public site shape, slugs, deletion, users, deactivation, dashboard, navigation and scope are all decided. Two things I'm assuming without having heard them from you:\n1. **Sanitizing:** Markdown is sanitized before it is rendered anywhere, including the console preview (per `CLAUDE.md`).\n2. **No ADRs.** Nothing here meets all three bars (hard to reverse, surprising, real trade-off) beyond ADR-001 and ADR-002. The nearest candidate is \"editors set Status, the Admin publishes\". It's easy to change later, so I'd keep it in the spec only. Agree?\n\nAnswer Q14 to Q16 and the two assumptions, then tell me explicitly whether we've reached a shared understanding. If so, I'll write the phase file as `spec` and you can run `/to-spec` in this same window."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":855,"cache_read_input_tokens":66164,"output_tokens":1266,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":855,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":1266,"cache_read_input_tokens":66164,"cache_creation_input_tokens":855,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":855},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"929f1b55-70ea-4749-9684-dfc094103409","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011Cg2zYP2vHGS65YjjV87bt","type":"assistant","uuid":"808026aa-8270-400e-91f4-1056e02523a1","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:25:12.568Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"f7eeb11f-ada1-4898-bc90-2ab1a08e71aa","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"e268a47c-4966-4488-a12a-66f5775df63c","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/.claude/skills/to-spec\n\nThis skill takes the current conversation context and codebase understanding and produces a spec. Do NOT interview the user; just synthesize what you already know.\n\nThe issue tracker and triage label vocabulary should have been provided to you. If not, tell the user to run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills`.\n\n## Process\n\n1. Explore the repo to understand the current state of the codebase, if you haven't already. Use the project's domain glossary vocabulary throughout the spec, and respect any ADRs in the area you're touching.\n\n2. Sketch out the seams at which you're going to test the feature. Existing seams should be preferred to new ones. Use the highest seam possible. If new seams are needed, propose them at the highest point you can. The fewer seams across the codebase, the better - the ideal number is one.\n\nCheck with the user that these seams match their expectations.\n\n3. Write the spec using the template below, then publish it to the project issue tracker. Apply the `ready-for-agent` triage label - no need for additional triage.\n\n<spec-template>\n\n## Problem Statement\n\nThe problem that the user is facing, from the user's perspective.\n\n## Solution\n\nThe solution to the problem, from the user's perspective.\n\n## User Stories\n\nA LONG, numbered list of user stories. Each user story should be in the format of:\n\n1. As an <actor>, I want a <feature>, so that <benefit>\n\n<user-story-example>\n1. As a mobile bank customer, I want to see balance on my accounts, so that I can make better informed decisions about my spending\n</user-story-example>\n\nThis list of user stories should be extremely extensive and cover all aspects of the feature.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\nA list of implementation decisions that were made. This can include:\n\n- The modules that will be built/modified\n- The interfaces of those modules that will be modified\n- Technical clarifications from the developer\n- Architectural decisions\n- Schema changes\n- API contracts\n- Specific interactions\n\nDo NOT include specific file paths or code snippets. They may end up being outdated very quickly.\n\nException: if a prototype produced a snippet that encodes a decision more precisely than prose can (state machine, reducer, schema, type shape), inline it within the relevant decision and note briefly that it came from a prototype. Trim to the decision-rich parts, not a working demo, just the important bits.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\nA list of testing decisions that were made. Include:\n\n- A description of what makes a good test (only test external behavior, not implementation details)\n- Which modules will be tested\n- Prior art for the tests (i.e. similar types of tests in the codebase)\n\n## Out of Scope\n\nA description of the things that are out of scope for this spec.\n\n## Further Notes\n\nAny further notes about the feature.\n\n</spec-template>\n"}]},"isMeta":true,"turnCompanion":true,"uuid":"6a8f775c-426d-4755-be03-53ba9745bf9a","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:27:52.336Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"e4508720-1c69-42bb-8258-2fc7bb7a89b2","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"e268a47c-4966-4488-a12a-66f5775df63c","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01D1fGWj7ye7BFCM5hj8AP1B","type":"tool_result","content":"# Issue tracker: GitHub\n\nIssues and specs for this repo live as GitHub issues. Use the `gh` CLI for all operations.\n\n## Conventions\n\n- **Create an issue**: `gh issue create --title \"...\" --body \"...\"`. Use a heredoc for multi-line bodies.\n- **Read an issue**: `gh issue view <number> --comments`, filtering comments by `jq` and also fetching labels.\n- **List issues**: `gh issue list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,comments --jq '[.[] | {number, title, body, labels: [.labels[].name], comments: [.comments[].body]}]'` with appropriate `--label` and `--state` filters.\n- **Comment on an issue**: `gh issue comment <number> --body \"...\"`\n- **Apply / remove labels**: `gh issue edit <number> --add-label \"...\"` / `--remove-label \"...\"`\n- **Close**: `gh issue close <number> --comment \"...\"`\n\nInfer the repo from `git remote -v`; `gh` does this automatically when run inside a clone.\n\n## Pull requests as a triage surface\n\n**PRs as a request surface: no.** _(Set to `yes` if this repo treats external PRs as feature requests; `/triage` reads this flag.)_\n\nWhen set to `yes`, PRs run through the same labels and states as issues, using the `gh pr` equivalents:\n\n- **Read a PR**: `gh pr view <number> --comments` and `gh pr diff <number>` for the diff.\n- **List external PRs for triage**: `gh pr list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,author,authorAssociation,comments` then keep only `authorAssociation` of `CONTRIBUTOR`, `FIRST_TIME_CONTRIBUTOR`, or `NONE` (drop `OWNER`/`MEMBER`/`COLLABORATOR`).\n- **Comment / label / close**: `gh pr comment`, `gh pr edit --add-label`/`--remove-label`, `gh pr close`.\n\nGitHub shares one number space across issues and PRs, so a bare `#42` may be either: resolve with `gh pr view 42` and fall back to `gh issue view 42`.\n\n## When a skill says \"publish to the issue tracker\"\n\nCreate a GitHub issue.\n\n## When a skill says \"fetch the relevant ticket\"\n\nRun `gh issue view <number> --comments`.\n\n## Wayfinding operations\n\nUsed by `/wayfinder`. The **map** is a single issue with **child** issues as tickets.\n\n- **Map**: a single issue labelled `wayfinder:map`, holding the Notes / Decisions-so-far / Fog body. `gh issue create --label wayfinder:map`.\n- **Child ticket**: an issue linked to the map as a GitHub sub-issue (`gh api` on the sub-issues endpoint). Where sub-issues aren't enabled, add the child to a task list in the map body and put `Part of #<map>` at the top of the child body. Labels: `wayfinder:<type>` (`research`/`prototype`/`grilling`/`task`). Once claimed, the ticket is assigned to the driving dev.\n- **Blocking**: GitHub's **native issue dependencies**, the canonical, UI-visible representation. Add an edge with `gh api --method POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<child>/dependencies/blocked_by -F issue_id=<blocker-db-id>`, where `<blocker-db-id>` is the blocker's numeric **database id** (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<n> --jq .id`, _not_ the `#number` or `node_id`). GitHub reports `issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by` (open blockers only, the live gate). Where dependencies aren't available, fall back to a `Blocked by: #<n>, #<n>` line at the top of the child body. A ticket is unblocked when every blocker is closed.\n- **Frontier query**: list the map's open children (`gh issue list --state open`, scoped to the map's sub-issues / task list), drop any with an open blocker (`issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by > 0`, or an open issue in the `Blocked by` line) or an assignee; first in map order wins.\n- **Claim**: `gh issue edit <n> --add-assignee @me`, the session's first write.\n- **Resolve**: `gh issue comment <n> --body \"<answer>\"`, then `gh issue close <n>`, then append a context pointer (gist + link) to the map's Decisions-so-far.\n# Triage Labels\n\nThe skills speak in terms of five canonical triage roles. This file maps those roles to the actual label strings used in this repo's issue tracker.\n\n| Label in mattpocock/skills | Label in our tracker | Meaning                                  |\n| -------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `needs-triage`             | `needs-triage`       | Maintainer needs to evaluate this issue  |\n| `needs-info`               | `needs-info`         | Waiting on reporter for more information |\n| `ready-for-agent`          | `ready-for-agent`    | Fully specified, ready for an AFK agent  |\n| `ready-for-human`          | `ready-for-human`    | Requires human implementation            |\n| `wontfix`                  | `wontfix`            | Will not be actioned                     |\n\nWhen a skill mentions a role (e.g. \"apply the AFK-ready triage label\"), use the corresponding label string from this table.\n\nEdit the right-hand column to match whatever vocabulary you actually use.\n__pycache__\nconftest.py\ntest_ctx_guard.py\ntest_t00.py\ntest_usage_report.py\n\"\"\"Shared test fixtures.\n\n`client` gives you the app. `client_as(role)` gives you a client that is logged\nin as a seeded user of that role — it works as soon as your login route exists,\nso access-control tests stay one line:\n\n    def test_editor_cannot_manage_users(client_as):\n        assert client_as(\"editor\").get(\"/admin/users\").status_code in (302, 403)\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport pytest\nfrom fastapi.testclient import TestClient\n\nfrom app.main import create_app\n\n# Matches scripts/seed_demo.py. Passwords come from the environment there; in\n# tests they are fixed and meaningless.\nDEMO_USERS = {\n    \"admin\": {\"email\": \"admin@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-admin-pw\"},\n    \"editor\": {\"email\": \"editor@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-editor-pw\"},\n}\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client() -> TestClient:\n    return TestClient(create_app())\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client_as():\n    \"\"\"Return a factory: client_as(\"editor\") -> a logged-in TestClient.\"\"\"\n\n    def _login(role: str) -> TestClient:\n        user = DEMO_USERS[role]\n        c = TestClient(create_app())\n        response = c.post(\"/login\", data={\"email\": user[\"email\"],\n                                          \"password\": user[\"password\"]},\n                          follow_redirects=False)\n        if response.status_code == 404:\n            pytest.skip(\"No /login route yet — build the login ticket first.\")\n        assert response.status_code in (200, 302, 303), (\n            f\"Login as {role} failed with {response.status_code}\")\n        return c\n\n    return _login\n   6 app/__init__.py\n  55 app/cli.py\n  42 app/main.py\n  47 app/publish.py\n  16 app/settings.py\n 166 total\nbug\tSomething isn't working\t#d73a4a\naccessibility\tBarrier affecting people with disabilities\t#f143ab\ndocumentation\tImprovements or additions to documentation\t#0075ca\nduplicate\tThis issue or pull request already exists\t#cfd3d7\nenhancement\tNew feature or request\t#a2eeef\ngood first issue\tGood for newcomers\t#7057ff\nhelp wanted\tExtra attention is needed\t#008672\ninvalid\tThis doesn't seem right\t#e4e669\nquestion\tFurther information is requested\t#d876e3\nwontfix\tThis will not be worked on\t#ffffff\njoehaugh/iphs400-mp2-cms","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"fd88ced9-366a-44cf-bcb5-2c5e8fed7842","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:27:57.551Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"# Issue tracker: GitHub\n\nIssues and specs for this repo live as GitHub issues. The skill wants your check on them before I write the spec, so I haven't published anything yet.\n\n**Proposed test seams (two, both existing):**\n\n1. **The HTTP seam.** I'd use the existing `client` / `client_as(role)` fixtures from `tests/conftest.py`. Every console rule is tested through real requests: login, roles, School scoping, Status changes, delete rules, user management, dashboard counts, preview, and slug handling. There's no test of models or helpers directly. The access-control tests (\"an Xavier Editor gets 404 on Fordham Prep's Post\") live here too.\n2. **The Publish seam.** The `cms publish` entry point runs against a seeded temporary database and writes to a temporary directory. Tests read the generated `site/` files, asserting on:\n   - only published content;\n   - sanitized Markdown;\n   - nav built from Pages (Pinned first);\n   - relative paths only;\n   - stale files removed.\n\nTwo changes to the fixtures are needed:\n- **Second School.** `client_as` currently knows one editor, so tests need a second Editor in a different School. Without it the cross-School rules can't be tested.\n- **Seed.** The seed needs a few Schools, plus the Network School.\n\nYou said Editors need the same rules enforced on the data and not just hidden in the UI, so tests on these two seams, not on SQL, are the right level.\n\nDo these match what you expect? A \"yes\" is enough, and I'll publish the spec.\n\nBefore I do, I need to create the `spec`, `ticket` and `ready-for-agent` labels on GitHub. {"parentUuid":"0a1e467a-388f-4bd3-a290-94269ecc092d","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"277199b2-8d02-45e9-939e-5901108bdbbd","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PqrivF4AEszo5rVMiyhsnR","type":"tool_result","content":"\"\"\"Render the public site into site/ as plain HTML.\n\nT00 publishes a placeholder home page. As you build content types, extend\nrender_site() to write one file per published item. Two rules the rubric checks:\n\n  1. Only PUBLISHED content is written here. A draft that reaches site/ is a bug.\n  2. Every href and src is RELATIVE (\"style.css\", \"posts/x.html\"), never\n     root-absolute (\"/style.css\"), because Pages serves this from a subfolder.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader, select_autoescape\n\nfrom app import settings\n\nCSS = \"\"\"/* Minimal starter styles — make them yours. */\n:root { color-scheme: light dark; }\nbody { font: 16px/1.6 system-ui, sans-serif; margin: 0 auto; max-width: 42rem; padding: 1rem; }\nheader a { font-weight: 700; text-decoration: none; }\nmain { margin-block: 2rem; }\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef environment() -> Environment:\n    return Environment(\n        loader=FileSystemLoader(str(settings.TEMPLATES)),\n        autoescape=select_autoescape([\"html\"]),\n    )\n\n\ndef render_site(out: Path | None = None) -> Path:\n    out = out or settings.SITE\n    if out.exists():\n        shutil.rmtree(out)\n    out.mkdir(parents=True)\n    env = environment()\n    (out / \"style.css\").write_text(CSS)\n    (out / \"index.html\").write_text(\n        env.get_template(\"public/home.html\").render(\n            title=settings.SITE_TITLE, items=[], css_path=\"style.css\",\n            home_path=\"index.html\",\n        )\n    )\n    return out\n\"\"\"The FastAPI application.\n\nT00 (already done): the admin console answers at /admin and the public site\nanswers at /. That is the whole skeleton — it exists so you can prove the stack\nruns before you build anything on it.\n\nAdd your routes in their own modules (app/routes/posts.py and so on) and include\nthem here. Keep this file small.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI, Request\nfrom fastapi.templating import Jinja2Templates\n\nfrom app import settings\n\ntemplates = Jinja2Templates(directory=str(settings.TEMPLATES))\n\n\ndef create_app() -> FastAPI:\n    app = FastAPI(title=\"IPHS 400 MP2 CMS\")\n\n    @app.get(\"/admin\")\n    def admin_home(request: Request):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"admin/hello.html\", {\"title\": \"Admin\"}\n        )\n\n    @app.get(\"/\")\n    def public_home(request: Request):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"public/home.html\",\n            {\"title\": settings.SITE_TITLE, \"items\": []},\n        )\n\n    # Your ticket work plugs in here, e.g.\n    #   from app.routes import posts\n    #   app.include_router(posts.router)\n    return app\n\n\napp = create_app()","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"fd1ce8a9-e46b-44ce-9f37-d9b4e5e669a4","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:28:35.463Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"\"\"\"Render the public site into site/ as plain HTML.\n\nT00 publishes a placeholder home page. agnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"b5a307cd-72de-4c32-981e-a6e97358eeb2","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011Cg2zp7kLw4VycsyTPaT3i","type":"assistant","uuid":"c980efe6-965c-4430-a0e0-20a746f65386","timestamp":"2026-10-14T23:28:37.159Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"c980efe6-965c-4430-a0e0-20a746f65386","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cg2zp7zypS7T9KyNNA5Vf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01MLvT99K7ucDXaGunnHRE8v","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/tmp/claude-1000/-home-joe-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9/scratchpad/spec.md","content":"## Problem Statement\n\nThe Jesuit Schools Network has about 60 member high schools. Their alumni news is scattered across social media accounts and buried on each school's own news page, so alumni connect with classmates but not with the wider network. Each school has one person (usually the head of alumni relations) who writes about their school. The network director, Jennifer LaMaster, supervises but does not write day to day. She needs one place where every School's Posts live together, with a guarantee that one School's Editor can never touch another School's content.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS in two halves, per ADR-001 and ADR-002.\n\n- A **local admin console** where the **Admin** (Jennifer) and **Editors** (one per School) manage **Posts** and **Pages**. Every Post and Page belongs to exactly one **School**; an Editor sees and changes only their own School's content.\n- A **public static site**, produced by **Publish**, showing a merged news feed of all Schools' published Posts, a Schools index, a page per School, and a navigation built from published Pages.\n\nThe CMS holds no records about **Alumni**. Alumni accounts, approval, and per-field privacy are out of scope because the public site is static and the console is never on the internet.\n\nVocabulary is defined in `CONTEXT.md` and used throughout.\n\n## User Stories\n\n**Login and sessions**\n1. As an Admin, I want to sign in with email and password, so that only authorised people can use the console.\n2. As an Editor, I want to sign in and out, so that my School's content is protected on a shared computer.\n3. As a User, I want a wrong password to be rejected with a plain message that does not say which part was wrong, so that accounts cannot be guessed.\n4. As a User with a temporary password, I want to be made to choose my own password at first login, so that the Admin no longer knows it.\n5. As a Deactivated User, I want my existing session to stop working immediately, so that a person who left cannot keep editing.\n6. As an Admin, I want passwords stored only as argon2 hashes and never logged, so that a stolen database does not reveal them.\n7. As a User, I want every form to carry a CSRF token, so that another website cannot make my browser change content.\n\n**Roles and Schools**\n8. As an Admin, I want the 60 Schools loaded from a seed list with city and state, so that I do not have to type them in.\n9. As an Admin, I want a Network School to exist alongside the 60, so that network-wide Pages like \"About\" and \"How to join\" have an owner.\n10. As an Editor, I want to be assigned to exactly one School, so that my permissions match my job.\n11. As an Editor, I want to see only my School's Posts and Pages, drafts included, so that I am not distracted by other Schools.\n12. As an Editor, I want a request for another School's Post or Page to look like it does not exist, so that I cannot probe what other Schools have.\n13. As an Admin, I want to act on every School's content, so that I can supervise and fix mistakes.\n14. As an Admin, I want only me to be able to write to the Network School, so that network-wide Pages are not changed by an Editor.\n\n**User management (Admin only)**\n15. As an Admin, I want to create Users and set a temporary password, so that Editors can start without email.\n16. As an Admin, I want to choose a School for each new Editor, so that their scope is correct from the first login.\n17. As an Admin, I want to change a User's role and reassign an Editor's School, so that staff changes are handled.\n18. As an Admin, I want content to stay with its School when an Editor is reassigned, so that nothing silently moves.\n19. As an Admin, I want to deactivate a User without deleting what they wrote, so that a School's history survives staff turnover.\n20. As an Editor, I want user-management screens to be unavailable to me, so that I cannot create accounts or change roles.\n\n**Posts**\n21. As an Editor, I want to create a Post with title, Markdown body, and Status, so that I can write news about my School.\n22. As an Editor, I want the address of a Post generated from its title, so that I never have to think about slugs.\n23. As an Editor, I want a duplicate address to be fixed for me by suggesting the next free one, so that I am not blocked by a rule I do not understand.\n24. As an Editor, I want a reserved address to be refused with a plain message, so that I cannot break the site's fixed pages.\n25. As an Editor, I want the Post to record who wrote it and when it was created and last changed, so that there is accountability.\n26. As an Editor, I want to edit a published Post in place, so that I can fix a typo without taking it offline.\n27. As an Editor, I want to delete my School's draft Posts, so that I can clean up.\n28. As an Editor, I want to set a published Post back to draft instead of deleting it, so that I can withdraw it safely.\n29. As an Admin, I want to permanently delete published content, so that removal of live content needs a second person.\n30. As an Alumnus, I want to see a Post labelled by School and not by author name, so that individual staff are not exposed.\n\n**Pages**\n31. As an Editor, I want to create and manage Pages for my School with the same rules as Posts, so that standing information is available.\n32. As an Admin, I want to pin Network School Pages at the front of the navigation, so that \"About\" and \"How to join\" come first.\n33. As an Alumnus, I want School Pages listed alphabetically after the pinned ones, so that I can find things predictably.\n\n**Admin console**\n34. As an Editor, I want a dashboard with counts of my School's draft and published Posts and Pages, so that I see my School's state at a glance.\n35. As an Admin, I want a dashboard for the whole Network with a per-School breakdown, so that I can see which Schools are not posting.\n36. As an Admin, I want a count of items changed since the last Publish, so that I know whether Publish is needed.\n37. As an Editor, I want a content list I can filter by Status and by type, so that I can find what to work on.\n38. As an Admin, I want to filter the content list by School as well, so that I can look at one School.\n39. As an Editor, I want a preview of my Markdown exactly as the public site will render it, sanitized, so that I know what alumni will see.\n\n**Publish and the public site**\n40. As an Admin, I want to run Publish to export all Schools' published content at once, so that I control what goes live.\n41. As an Editor, I want setting a Post's Status to published to mean \"ready\", not \"live\", so that I understand it appears at the next Publish.\n42. As an Alumnus, I want a home page with a merged feed of every School's published Posts, newest first, so that I see the whole network in one place.\n43. As an Alumnus, I want a Schools index listing all 60 Schools as plain text, so that I can find mine without using a map.\n44. As an Alumnus, I want a page per School listing its published Posts and Pages, so that I can follow my own School.\n45. As an Alumnus, I want the site to contain only published content, so that unfinished work is never visible.\n46. As an Alumnus, I want unpublished or deleted items to disappear at the next Publish, so that withdrawn content stays withdrawn.\n47. As an Alumnus, I want Markdown rendered safely, with scripts stripped, so that the site cannot attack me.\n48. As an Alumnus, I want images linked from other sites to display in Posts, so that school news can have photos.\n49. As an Alumnus, I want every link on the site to work when it is served from a subfolder, so that nothing is broken on the hosting platform.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n**Domain model** (terms as in `CONTEXT.md`)\n- **School**: name, short name, city, state, and a stable URL name. About 60 rows plus the **Network School**. Loaded from a seed list; there is no console screen to add, edit, or remove Schools.\n- **User**: email, argon2 password hash, role (admin or editor), active flag, and a \"must change password\" flag. An Editor has exactly one School; an Admin has none.\n- **Post** and **Page**: School, title, address (slug), Markdown body, Status (draft or published), author, created and updated timestamps. Pages that belong to the Network School also carry a pin order. A \"changed since last Publish\" comparison needs the time of the last Publish to be recorded.\n- Slugs are unique per School, separately for Posts and for Pages.\n\n**Permissions** (enforced in data access, never only in templates)\n- Editor queries are always filtered to their School. A Post or Page of another School returns not-found for every route (view, edit, delete, status change).\n- Editors may create, edit, and delete their School's draft content, and may set published content back to draft. Only the Admin may permanently delete published content.\n- Only the Admin may manage Users, reassign Schools, write Network School content, pin Pages, and run Publish.\n- A User is checked against the database on each request, so deactivation and role changes take effect immediately.\n- No route may rely on the client to name the School; the School comes from the signed-in User, or from the Admin's explicit choice.\n\n**Public site structure** (all paths relative)\n- Home: merged feed of published Posts across Schools, newest first, each labelled with its School.\n- Schools index: all 60 Schools as an alphabetical text list.\n- Per-School page: that School's published Posts and Pages.\n- Individual content at `<school>/<slug>.html`; Posts and Pages share the School's folder, so a School's Post and Page cannot share a slug. This rule is a decision to settle in the data model ticket.\n- Navigation: Pinned Pages first in the Admin's order, then the Schools entry. School Pages appear on the School's own page, not in the top-level nav, so 60 Schools do not clutter it.\n- A reserved slug list protects fixed paths (the home, the Schools index, and the like).\n- Byline shows the School only.\n\n**Publish**\n- Rebuilds the entire output from scratch on every run (existing behaviour of the render function), writing only published rows. Removed or unpublished items disappear.\n- Records the time of the last Publish for the dashboard.\n- Markdown is sanitized before it is rendered anywhere (console preview and public). Script and event-handler content is stripped. External image links are allowed; relative-path rules apply only to the site's own links.\n\n**Console**\n- Dashboards: Editor sees own-School counts (Posts and Pages, draft and published, changed since last Publish). Admin sees Network counts plus a per-School breakdown.\n- Content list filters: Status and type for everyone; School for the Admin.\n- Preview renders the same sanitized HTML as the public site.\n- Forms carry CSRF tokens. Passwords are argon2-hashed; a new User must change a temporary password at first login.\n- Plain-language error messages for slug problems (duplicate suggests the next free address; reserved is refused).\n\n**Sequencing hint for tickets**: each ticket should be a thin vertical slice (model, route, template, test) that is demoable alone, for example login and roles with a School-scoped list, then Post create and edit, then Page and nav, then Publish, then users, then dashboard and filters.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n- **Good tests assert external behaviour**: HTTP responses, redirects, what appears in the rendered pages, and the files Publish writes. They do not assert on internal functions or SQL.\n- **Seam 1, HTTP**: the existing `client` and `client_as(role)` fixtures. Needs extending with a second School's Editor so cross-School rules are testable, and a seed with several Schools plus the Network School.\n- **Seam 2, Publish**: run Publish against a seeded temporary database into a temporary directory, then read the generated files. Assertions: only published content present; drafts absent; sanitized Markdown; nav built from Pinned Pages; all href and src values relative; unpublished items removed on the next run.\n- Required cross-School tests, per route (view, edit, delete, status change): an Editor of one School gets not-found on another's Post and Page.\n- Prior art: `tests/test_t00.py` (HTTP smoke test) and the module docstring in `tests/conftest.py`.\n- Read each test `/tdd` writes before letting it pass: a test written against a misunderstanding goes green.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Alumni accounts, directory, approval, and per-field privacy (phone, email, occupation). The CMS stores no Alumni data.\n- A US map of Schools. This is a non-functional mockup outside `site/`, with made-up data, only after the CMS works.\n- Image uploads and a media library (external image links are allowed).\n- Comments, search, tags, categories.\n- Scheduled publishing and revision history.\n- Rich-text editing and page builders (excluded by the assignment).\n- Email, password reset by email, and any live deployment of the admin console.\n- Adding, editing, or removing Schools from the console.\n- Multi-site.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- No new ADRs: nothing beyond ADR-001 and ADR-002 is both hard to reverse and a real trade-off. The Status-versus-Publish split lives here.\n- Known consequences to explain to the client: a published item is live only at the next Publish; unpublishing breaks any old links to it; the public site is open to anyone (no reader login).\n- Editing a published Post changes the stored copy immediately; there is no separate working copy.\n- The seed file of 60 Schools needs real data from the client; use made-up placeholders for demos.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1316,"cache_read_input_tokens":74314,"output_tokens":4608,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":59},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1316,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":4608,"cache_read_input_tokens":74314,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1316,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1316},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_01MLvT99K7ucDXaGunnHRE8v":{"file_path":"/tmp/claude-1000/-home-joe-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/d0a23431-29b1-4338-8f58-72449daa24f9/scratchpad/spec.md","content":"## Problem Statement\n\nThe Jesuit Schools Network has about 60 member high schools.
